# Raw Multi-Agent Findings (evidence)
Each lane: investigation findings + an independent adversarial re-check of its critical/high findings. Verdicts: confirmed / refuted / corrected / overstated / understated.

---

## Lane A1-addrow-insert — readiness 68/100
**Summary:** The audit's "Add Row Flow" section is highly accurate: every cited file:line for the insert path (drop-zones.tsx:36, EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1960/220/1700, use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:156, actions.ts:2493) and all three cited tests verify exactly as described, behavior included. The plan correctly understands that Add row reuses the library-insert path with a blank placeholder template. The CRITICAL feasibility question for U2 resolves in two parts: (a) the DB RPC insert_estimate_row_item_with_history ALREADY returns the persisted row plus row_revision (migration line 396-401, identical shape to update/detach), but (b) the app action insertEstimateRowItemFromLibrary discards it — it destructures only { error }, returns void, and the insert mutation's shared onSuccess never reconciles the optimistic row's client id/revision into cache. So the "buffer commits until persisted row readiness + row revision" contract is FEASIBLE with an app-layer-only change (no DB migration), which is a materially smaller lift than a naive reading suggests — but the plan never states this, and U2 leaves the revision-threading and the insert-onSuccess reconciliation unspecified. Two real gaps the plan underplays: the failed-insert path currently DELETES the optimistic row via rollback (so "keep a draft row with Retry/Delete" requires replacing rollback-on-error, not adding to it), and the server RPC hard-codes event_type='row_blank_added' whenever source_library_item_id is null, which means express rows are recorded as blank-added in the events table unless a DB migration changes the classifier — contradicting U2's "History contract" caveat that this is an app/composer choice.

**Dimension scores:** Ground-truth accuracy of cited file:line claims 95; Insert-return / row_revision feasibility (the critical U2 dependency) 70; Pending-insert / draft-recovery completeness 62; History/event-type contract correctness 55; Test impact awareness 78

**Strengths:**
- Every audited file:line in the 'Add Row Flow' section is correct to the exact line, including drop-zones.tsx:36, EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1960/220/1700, use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:156, and actions.ts:2493 — line numbers were not trusted and all confirmed against commit 72628d46.
- The behavioral descriptions are precise: the audit correctly notes the optimistic path clones the row locally (newItem) but passes the original libraryItem to insertRow (EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1706-1727), and correctly identifies linkState derivation (actions.ts:2552).
- All three cited tests exist and assert exactly what the audit claims (library-insert.test.tsx:247 unlinked manual template; cost-inputs.test.tsx:209-216 keyboard slot creation; :230 persisted placeholder rendering).
- The plan's idempotency-by-correlation design (U2 Retry reuses correlation) is backed by real infrastructure: the RPC short-circuits via estimate_sheet_event_result(correlation_id) and the client threads a mutationId->correlation_id, so duplicate-free retry is genuinely achievable.
- KTD2/R2 are sound: estimate_row_items + metadata.expressComponent reuses the existing insert RPC and snapshot/revision/history machinery without a new table or enum.

**Findings:**

### [HIGH] A1-F1 — Insert app action discards the row_revision the DB RPC already returns — core U2 dependency is feasible but unstated  _(type: missing-evidence)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-implementation-plan.md U2 (lines 72-75), R10 (line 28), AE2a (line 124)
- **Plan claim:** U2 Details: 'Insert must return enough persisted row data, including row revision, for subsequent updates to be enabled.' The pending-insert contract buffers express field commits until insert success returns persisted row readiness and row revision (R10, AE2a).
- **Ground truth:** The DB RPC insert_estimate_row_item_with_history ALREADY returns { idempotent, eventId, rowId, row: estimate_row_item_snapshot(inserted_row) } including row_revision (migration 20260524033151:396-401), the same shape readHistoryRowResult consumes for update/detach. But the app action insertEstimateRowItemFromLibrary destructures only `{ error }` (actions.ts:2530), returns void (no return statement, ends 2558-2559), and the insert mutation uses the shared mutationOptions whose onSuccess takes `(_result: void, ...)` and never setQueryData-reconciles the optimistic row (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:142-146, 156-159). So the optimistic client row keeps its client-generated id and has NO rowRevision after success.
- **Detail:** This is the single most important feasibility result for U2. The contract the plan depends on is achievable WITHOUT a DB migration: the action only needs to switch to `const { data, error } = await supabase.rpc(...)`, call readHistoryRowResult(data) (already exists, actions.ts:337), change its return type from void to UpdateEstimateRowItemResult (or a new InsertResult), and the insert mutation's onSuccess must setQueryData to patch rowRevision (and ideally reconcile id) into the optimistic row. The plan correctly senses the requirement but neither states that the DB already supplies the value nor calls out the three concrete code changes, leaving an estimator/implementer to assume a larger (possibly DB-level) change. Marking missing-evidence/high because the whole pending-insert lifecycle (R10, AE2a) rests on this and the plan presents it as an open requirement rather than a near-trivial wiring change.
- **Evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:396, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2530, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2558, src/hooks/use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:142, src/hooks/use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:156, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2493
- **Fix:** In U2, state explicitly: the insert RPC already returns row+row_revision (cite migration:396-401); the only changes are (1) read `data` in insertEstimateRowItemFromLibrary and return readHistoryRowResult(data); (2) widen InsertEstimateRowItemFromLibraryInput return type; (3) add an insert-specific onSuccess that patches rowRevision/id into the cached optimistic row via setQueryData (mirroring detachMutation onSuccess at use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:199-207). No migration is needed for revision return.
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→high) — Independently confirmed on every load-bearing claim. (1) The insert RPC insert_estimate_row_item_with_history returns jsonb_build_object('idempotent', false, 'eventId', inserted_event_id, 'rowId', inserted_row.id, 'row', public.estimate_row_item_snapshot(inserted_row)) at actions migration lines 396-401 — identical shape to update/detach. (2) estimate_row_item_snapshot DOES include 'rowRevision', row_item.row_revision (migration line 150), and row_revision is a NOT NULL column (migration line 9), so the persisted row_revision is genuinely in the insert payload already — no DB migration needed, exactly as the finding states. (3) readHistoryRowResult exists at actions.ts:337 and reads rowRevision off result.row.rowRevision (line 356), already used by updateEstimateRowItem (line 2582) and the detach path (line 2645). (4) The bug is real: insertEstimateRowItemFromLibrary destructures ONLY `{ error: insertError }` (actions.ts:2530), never reads `data`, and the function has no return statement — it ends at line 2558-2559 with `if (insertError) throw insertError;` and an implicit void return (return type is inferred void; contrast updateEstimateRowItem at 2561 / detach at 2628 which are explicitly typed Promise<UpdateEstimateRowItemResult>). (5) The insert mutation (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:156-159) spreads the shared mutationOptions whose onSuccess signature is `(_result: void, input: RollbackInput)` (line 142) and does ONLY markSaved + invalidateQueries with refetchType:'none' (lines 143-145) — it never setQueryData-reconciles the optimistic row, so the client row keeps its client-generated id (newItem.id from cloneLibraryItemForEstimate, EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1706) and has no rowRevision after success. Compare update/detach onSuccess at lines 199/247 which DO consume `result: UpdateEstimateRowItemResult`. The remediation described (switch to `const { data, error }`, call readHistoryRowResult(data), change return type, patch via setQueryData) is accurate and the three concrete changes are correctly scoped. Severity high is appropriate: U2's entire pending-insert/row-revision contract rests on this, and the plan frames it as an open requirement rather than the near-trivial wiring it is. Note: persistedRowId (actions.ts:2528) reuses the client rowId when it is a valid UUID, so id reconciliation is usually a no-op in practice — a minor nuance the finding flags only as 'ideally', which is fair.

### [HIGH] A1-F2 — Failed insert currently DELETES the optimistic row; 'keep a draft row with Retry/Delete' replaces, not extends, existing behavior  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-implementation-plan.md U2 (line 73), R3 (line 21), AE2a (line 124)
- **Plan claim:** U2 Pending-insert contract: 'If insert fails, keep a draft row with Retry and Delete actions. Retry reuses the same client row id or mutation correlation so duplicates are not created.' AE2a: 'if insert fails, a draft recovery UI remains visible.'
- **Ground truth:** The insert mutation uses shared mutationOptions whose onError calls input.rollback?.() (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:147-151). For inserts, rollback is restoreEstimateState(previousTree, previousRows, rollbackRevision) (EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1728), which restores the tree to BEFORE the row was appended — i.e. the optimistically-inserted row is removed on failure. There is currently no draft-preserving failure state for inserts.
- **Detail:** Today a failed Add row makes the row vanish (rollback). The plan's draft-recovery contract therefore requires REPLACING the insert onError rollback with a path that (a) keeps the optimistic row in the tree, (b) marks it as a failed draft, (c) preserves the mutationId for idempotent retry, and (d) only deletes on explicit user action. The plan describes the desired end-state but does not flag that the existing rollback-on-error is the thing being removed, nor where the draft state and mutationId would be stored (the optimistic insert at EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1700-1731 does not retain the mutationId — withInsertMutationIdentity generates it inside the hook at use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:284-289, so it is not visible to the component for retry).
- **Evidence:** src/hooks/use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:147, src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1728, src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1704
- **Fix:** U2 should specify: (1) give inserts a dedicated onMutate/onError instead of shared mutationOptions, so failure keeps the row and flags a draft instead of calling rollback; (2) surface a stable mutationId to the component (e.g. generate it in EstimateSheetContent before calling insertRow) so Retry can resend the SAME correlation_id and hit the RPC's estimate_sheet_event_result idempotency short-circuit (migration:303-306); (3) define where draft/failed-insert state lives (saveState is currently global, not per-row for inserts).
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→high) — Independently confirmed. (1) The insert mutation spreads mutationOptions whose onError calls `input.rollback?.()` then markFailed + invalidate (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:147-151). (2) For inserts, the rollback passed from the component is `() => restoreEstimateState(previousTree, previousRows, rollbackRevision)` (EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1728). previousTree is captured at line 1704 BEFORE the optimistic row is appended at lines 1708-1718, so restoreEstimateState (defined at 1634-1639, which calls restoreEstimateTree(previousTree)) reverts to the pre-append tree — i.e. the optimistically-inserted row IS removed on failure. So today a failed Add makes the row vanish; there is no draft-preserving failure state. The plan's 'keep a draft row with Retry/Delete' (AE2a) therefore REPLACES this rollback-on-error path, as the finding asserts, not extends it. (3) The mutationId-visibility claim is confirmed: insertLibraryItem's insertRow call (EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1722-1729) does NOT pass a mutationId; withInsertMutationIdentity generates it inside the hook via createMutationId() at use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:287 (createMutationId defined at line 97), so the id never surfaces to the component and cannot be reused for idempotent retry without a wiring change. The original finding cited 'use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:284-289' for withInsertMutationIdentity — confirmed exactly (lines 284-289). The optimistic-insert range '1700-1731' is also exact (insertLibraryItem spans 1700-1731). One small note: the finding says rollback 'restores the tree to BEFORE the row was appended' — precisely correct, and worth adding that the idempotent retry is partly already supported server-side (the RPC accepts correlation_id and getMutationCorrelationId is wired at actions.ts:2555), so the missing piece is purely client-side: surfacing/persisting the mutationId and replacing onError. Severity high is justified — this is a behavioral inversion (delete vs. preserve) of the existing flow that the plan does not flag.

### [MEDIUM] A1-F3 — Server RPC hard-codes event_type='row_blank_added' for any null source id — express rows are recorded as blank-added unless a migration changes it  _(type: inconsistency)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-implementation-plan.md U2 (line 74), U8 (line 117)
- **Plan claim:** U2 History contract: 'The insert history path must classify only legacy blank placeholders as row_blank_added. Express inserts use the existing row_added activity unless a new composer-backed event is added.' U8 echoes 'history titles do not call express rows blank rows'.
- **Ground truth:** The insert RPC sets event_type via `case when inserted_row.source_library_item_id is null then 'row_blank_added' else 'row_added' end` (migration 20260524033151:384; same in 20260524050746:422). The P6 relax migration (20260614013638) only strips the node.kind guard — it does NOT touch the event-type classifier. Express rows are project-scoped with source_library_item_id = null, so the server will classify EVERY express insert as 'row_blank_added'.
- **Detail:** The event_type is decided in the RPC, not in the app or the activity composer. So the plan's 'unless a new composer-backed event is added' framing is inaccurate for the persisted event row: classifying express inserts as row_added (or a new row_express_added) requires a DB migration to the insert RPC's CASE expression (e.g. branch on metadata.expressComponent or on link_state), not merely an app-side composer change. The audit's 'History contract' caveat understates this by implying it is an app-layer choice. The activity-history composer (activity-history.tsx:121-131) can re-label by material/tag downstream, but the underlying event_type in estimate_sheet_events will still be row_blank_added unless the RPC changes — which matters for any consumer that reads event_type directly.
- **Evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:384, supabase/migrations/20260524050746_add_estimate_row_contingency_percentage.sql:422, supabase/migrations/20260614013638_enable_estimate_node_mixed_writes_p6.sql:55
- **Fix:** In U2/U8 and KTD7, state that distinguishing express inserts from blank placeholders at the event level requires modifying the insert RPC's event_type CASE (cite migration:384) to consider metadata.expressComponent (or a non-null marker), and decide whether to add a 'row_express_added' event_type or reuse 'row_added'. List supabase/migrations/* as a required (not 'only if needed') file for U2's history contract.

### [LOW] A1-F4 — Audit cites the superseded version-control migration; the effective insert RPC was relaxed by a later P6 migration  _(type: inaccuracy)_
- **Plan loc:** 01-current-state-audit.md (line 52), implicitly U2/U3
- **Plan claim:** Audit: 'supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:478 already supports sourceSnapshot and metadata in the underlying row patch.' Plan generally references the version-control migration as the RPC source of truth.
- **Ground truth:** The insert RPC is defined in 20260524033151:278 but is later redefined by 20260524050746 (contingency) and RELAXED at runtime by 20260614013638_enable_estimate_node_mixed_writes_p6.sql, which strips the `and node.kind = 'area'` guard from insert_estimate_row_item_with_history via pg_get_functiondef regexp rewrite. The base migration still RAISES 'Target area node not found' (line 308-316); only the P6 migration removes that gate.
- **Detail:** Line 52's specific claim about migration :478 is in the update RPC region and is directionally fine for U3, but anyone reasoning about the insert RPC from the version-control migration alone will incorrectly believe inserts are still gated to kind='area'. The audit's own EstimateSheetContent comment quote (actions.ts:2499-2502) about 'a row may be inserted into ANY node' is only true because of the P6 migration, which the audit does not cite. Low severity because it does not change U2's design, but it affects which migration file an implementer edits for U2's history-contract change (must edit the effective definition, and the P6 migration mutates functions in place).
- **Evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260614013638_enable_estimate_node_mixed_writes_p6.sql:57, supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:308, supabase/migrations/20260524050746_add_estimate_row_contingency_percentage.sql:349
- **Fix:** Add a note that the live insert RPC is the version-control definition AS RELAXED by 20260614013638 (no kind gate), and that any RPC body change for the express history-event classifier must account for that in-place rewrite migration so a fresh-env definition and hosted definition stay consistent.

### [MEDIUM] A1-F5 — First express update can hit 'row not found' if it races ahead of insert commit — the exact race the buffer contract must close  _(type: risk)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-implementation-plan.md R10 (line 28), AE2a (line 124), U2 (line 73)
- **Plan claim:** AE2a/R10: field edits may focus immediately but server commits wait for persisted row readiness; buffered field commit runs after persisted row revision is known.
- **Ground truth:** updateEstimateRowItem calls getValidatedEstimateRow(projectId, estimateId, rowId) which SELECTs estimate_row_items by id (actions.ts:686-693, 2562). If an express update fires before the insert RPC commits the row, that SELECT returns no row and the action throws 'row not found'. expected_row_revision falls back to the freshly-read row.row_revision (actions.ts:2575) only IF the row exists, so a first update after commit is safe from optimistic-lock failure, but a first update BEFORE commit fails outright.
- **Detail:** This confirms the buffer contract is genuinely necessary (not gold-plating): without gating on insert success, a fast type-then-blur produces an update against a not-yet-persisted row id and errors. The good news: the insert mutation exposes onSuccess (the row is committed by then), so buffering is implementable by holding express field commits until the insert mutation resolves successfully. The plan's design is therefore sound; this finding documents the precise failure mode and confirms the dependency, and notes a secondary benefit of returning revision (F1): carrying an exact expectedRowRevision avoids a lost-update window if a concurrent editor bumps the revision between insert commit and first buffered update.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:686, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2562, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2575
- **Fix:** Keep R10/AE2a as designed; in U2 reference the concrete mechanism (gate buffered commits on insertMutation success state) and note getValidatedEstimateRow throws on a missing row (actions.ts:686) as the failure being prevented. Add a test that a buffered edit dispatched pre-commit does not fire an update until after insert onSuccess.

### [INFO] A1-F6 — Optimistic clone uses cloneLibraryItemForEstimate; the audit/plan correctly note the original template (not the clone) is sent to the action  _(type: strength)_
- **Plan loc:** 01-current-state-audit.md (line 16), 04 Sources (line 133)
- **Plan claim:** Audit: EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1700 'optimistically clones and appends the row, then calls insertRow with the original template payload.'
- **Ground truth:** insertLibraryItem (EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1700-1731): newItem = cloneLibraryItemForEstimate(libraryItem) is appended to the tree and used for setPendingFocusRowId/getInitialEditableRow, but insertRow receives rowId: newItem.id AND the ORIGINAL libraryItem (line 1726-1727). The server then re-derives id from rowId (persistedRowId = isUuid(rowId) ? rowId : randomUUID(), actions.ts:2528).
- **Detail:** Accurate and important: the clone supplies the client row id (newItem.id) while the original template supplies field values. This split is exactly why the plan's express template must set source:'project' and express metadata on the template object passed to insertLibraryItem so it survives both the clone (for display) and the action payload (for persistence via getEstimateRowMetadata/getSourceSnapshot at actions.ts:2551/2526). Recording as a strength because the audit got a subtle two-object flow right, which de-risks U2's template swap.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1706, src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1726, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2528
- **Fix:** No change needed. When U2 introduces createExpressEstimateRowTemplate(), ensure express metadata is set on the template (so getEstimateRowMetadata at actions.ts:2551 persists it) and verify cloneLibraryItemForEstimate preserves metadata/tags on the optimistic clone.

## Lane A2-material-cell-virtualization — readiness 58/100
**Summary:** I ground-truthed every cited claim in the "Material Cell Shape" and "Layout Constraints / Inline Material Cell" sections against the code at HEAD 72628d46. The factual audit is strong: cells.tsx and row-table.tsx line citations are correct or off by only a few lines with the right symbol/behavior, and the "display-only material cell" claim is fully verified (EditableRowValue exposes only quantity/contingencyMode/contingencyPercentage/miscCost; no material/description/unit/itemCost/supplier editing exists). The serious problem is virtualization: the estimate row virtualizer is a FIXED-height virtualizer (estimateSize: () => 96, no measureElement), and rows are absolutely positioned at a fixed 96px stride (translateY(index*96 - scrollMargin)) once virtualization is active. The plan never identifies this mechanism. Sibling virtualizers (tree.tsx, virtual-branch-list.tsx) DO use measureElement for dynamic heights, making the row-table's fixed-height design a deliberate, load-bearing asymmetry the plan misses entirely. The plan's mitigation ("Define stable active row height and verify with browser smoke," RSK7/Medium) is hand-wavy and underscoped for what is actually a hard layout constraint: any active express editor taller than 96px will overlap the next row whenever there are >=75 rows and the flag is on. There is also a minor field-naming inconsistency (unit_cost vs itemCost) across plan docs.

**Dimension scores:** Factual accuracy of cited code (line numbers + behavior) 85; Virtualization understanding / correctness 35; Layout-constraint concreteness / actionability 45; QA / verification coverage for this slice 40; Internal consistency across plan docs 75

**Strengths:**
- Audit line citations in 01-current-state-audit.md 'Material Cell Shape' are accurate to the symbol: cells.tsx:179 (isBlankEstimateRowItem branch), :217 (action rail div), :276 (Project-specific badge), row-table.tsx:61 (comparator), :88 (ROW_COLUMNS with minmax(160px,1fr)), :212 (EstimateMaterialCell td) all verified exact or within 1 line.
- The core 'display-first / not editable inline' claim (audit line 28) is fully correct: EditableRowValue in editable-row-model.ts:44-49 contains only quantity, contingencyMode, contingencyPercentage, miscCost — confirming no inline name/description/unit/unit_cost/supplier editing exists today.
- The plan correctly identifies that the material column uses minmax(160px,1fr) and correctly anchors the layout-pressure concern to row-table.tsx:88 (03-ux-interaction-design.md:90).
- The plan does name the virtualization risk at all (RSK7, QA matrix line 81, Layout Constraints line 94-96) rather than ignoring it — a genuine if shallow awareness.
- The commit/HEAD provenance is honest: HEAD is 72628d46 as stated, and the recent commit 0e5d3ddb did introduce TanStack Virtual, so the plan's framing of 'recent virtualization' is accurate.

**Findings:**

### [CRITICAL] A2-F1 — Plan misses that the estimate row virtualizer is FIXED-height (estimateSize: () => 96, no measureElement) — an expanding express editor will overlap the next row  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 03-ux-interaction-design.md:93-96 (Layout Constraints); 06-review-and-risk-register.md:23 (RSK7); 05-readiness-qa-matrix.md:81
- **Plan claim:** "Keep row height stable enough for virtualization" and "Allow active edit state to expand only within defined row height rules, or disable virtualization assumptions for active rows." RSK7 mitigation: "Define stable active row height and verify with browser smoke." QA: "Row virtualization may mismeasure active express editor height."
- **Ground truth:** The row virtualizer uses a FIXED size estimator and never measures actual DOM height. row-table.tsx:421 `estimateSize: () => virtualization.estimateRowHeight ?? 96`; there is NO `measureElement` ref anywhere in row-table.tsx (grep confirms only estimateSize + a scrollMargin ResizeObserver). When virtualEnabled, each row is absolutely positioned at a fixed stride: row-table.tsx:594-600 sets `position:absolute; transform: translateY(${virtualRow.start - scrollMargin}px)` where virtualRow.start = index * 96 (row-table.tsx:502 in the SSR fallback and TanStack's fixed-size calc). The container height is `rowVirtualizer.getTotalSize()` (row-table.tsx:566), computed from the 96px estimate. Therefore any active express editor that grows taller than 96px does NOT push siblings down — the next row stays pinned at (index+1)*96 and the taller row's content overflows/overlaps it. The row class is `min-h-24` (=96px) (row-table.tsx:185), so non-virtual flow CAN grow, but virtual mode cannot. This is not 'mismeasurement' (RSK7's framing); the heights are never measured at all.
- **Detail:** The plan treats virtualization as a soft 'keep height stable' nicety and frames the failure as 'mismeasure' (implying TanStack measures and might get it wrong). In reality there is no measurement: the virtualizer is hard fixed-stride at 96px. An express editor with a multi-line description textarea, an inline error message (aria-describedby target), and an image set/clear control will easily exceed 96px. Once items.length >= 75 (virtualization.minRows default, row-table.tsx:417) and NEXT_PUBLIC_ESTIMATE_ROW_VIRTUALIZATION is on (default true, virtualization-flags.ts:13), an active editor will visually overlap the following row, and the total scroll height will be wrong (short by the overflow). This is the single biggest implementation-readiness gap in this slice and is mislabeled as Medium severity.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:421, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:566, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:594, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:598, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:185
- **Fix:** Add an explicit virtualization-aware design decision to U4 and RSK7. Pick ONE concrete strategy and write it into the plan: (a) wire `ref={rowVirtualizer.measureElement}` + `data-index` onto the row <tr> (matching tree.tsx:1281 / virtual-branch-list.tsx:143) so TanStack dynamically measures the active editor — note this also requires removing/adjusting the SSR fixed-stride fallback at row-table.tsx:491-505 and the `[contain-intrinsic-size:96px]`/`min-h-24` assumptions; OR (b) hard-cap the active editor to <=96px (scroll/clamp the description, no inline expansion), keeping fixed-stride valid; OR (c) when a row enters active-edit, force virtualization off for that table (set virtualEnabled false or scrollToIndex + render-full) — but spell out the >=75-row case where that regresses perf. 'Disable virtualization assumptions for active rows' (line 96) is not actionable as written.
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→critical) — Independently verified every concrete claim against the source. row-table.tsx:421 uses a FIXED estimator `estimateSize: () => virtualization.estimateRowHeight ?? 96`; grep for `measureElement` in row-table.tsx returns NONE FOUND (no dynamic measurement anywhere). In virtual mode each <tr> is absolutely positioned at a fixed stride: row-table.tsx:594-600 sets `position:absolute; top:0; transform: translateY(${virtualRow.start - scrollMargin}px)`, and the SSR/initial fallback computes `start: index * virtualEstimateRowHeight` where virtualEstimateRowHeight = 96 (row-table.tsx:487, used at :502). The <tbody> height is `rowVirtualizer.getTotalSize()` (row-table.tsx:566), derived from the 96px estimate. The row class is `min-h-24` (=96px) at row-table.tsx:185 — a MINIMUM, so the non-virtual flow can grow but the absolute-positioned virtual layout cannot reflow siblings. Conclusion holds exactly: a >96px active editor overlaps the next row and totalSize is short by the overflow. The finding's reframing is precise and correct: this is NOT 'mismeasurement' (RSK7/QA wording implies TanStack measures and errs) — heights are never measured at all. RSK7 is labeled 'Medium' at 06-review-and-risk-register.md:23 and the QA 'Release Risks' bullet says 'mismeasure' at 05-readiness-qa-matrix.md:81, both of which understate/mischaracterize the defect. Critical severity is justified given the flag defaults on (virtualization-flags.ts:13) and the >=75-row gate (row-table.tsx:417). One nuance the finding omitted but does not change the verdict: line 185 also carries `[content-visibility:auto] [contain-intrinsic-size:96px]`, which only affects off-screen render cost, not the absolute-stride overlap.

### [HIGH] A2-F2 — Plan does not note that sibling virtualizers DO use measureElement, making row-table's fixed-height a deliberate asymmetry the express editor must respect  _(type: missing-evidence)_
- **Plan loc:** 01-current-state-audit.md:37-41
- **Plan claim:** "The row table memoization and virtualization depend on stable item identities." The audit characterizes virtualization only via the comparator and grid, never via the height model.
- **Ground truth:** tree.tsx:600 uses `estimateSize: () => 28` but ALSO attaches `ref={rowVirtualizer.measureElement}` at tree.tsx:1281 (dynamic measurement). virtual-branch-list.tsx:98 uses `estimateSize: () => 192` and `ref={branchVirtualizer.measureElement}` at virtual-branch-list.tsx:143 (dynamic measurement). row-table.tsx has neither — it is the ONLY one of the three workshop virtualizers that is purely fixed-stride. So the codebase already has the measureElement pattern available and proven; the row-table simply opted out (likely because uniform 96px rows made it unnecessary).
- **Detail:** This is decisive context the plan omits: making variable-height express rows work under virtualization is a SOLVED pattern elsewhere in this exact repo. The implementation does not need to invent measurement — it needs to port the measureElement wiring already present in tree.tsx and virtual-branch-list.tsx into row-table.tsx. Omitting this makes the plan's RSK7 read as an open research risk when it is actually a known, bounded code change. Conversely it raises a subtlety: the row uses CSS-grid <tr> rows with absolute positioning and a scrollMargin offset that the tree/branch lists may handle differently, so a straight copy is not guaranteed — that interaction must be validated.
- **Evidence:** src/components/ui/tree.tsx:600, src/components/ui/tree.tsx:1281, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/virtual-branch-list.tsx:98, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/virtual-branch-list.tsx:143, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:421
- **Fix:** Cite tree.tsx:1281 and virtual-branch-list.tsx:143 in the plan as the reference implementation for dynamic row measurement, and in U4 explicitly require porting the measureElement + data-index pattern (or justify why a fixed cap is chosen instead). Note the row-table specific complications (absolute positioning, scrollMargin at row-table.tsx:564-566, contain-intrinsic-size/content-visibility at row-table.tsx:185) that differ from the tree's flow.
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→high) — Verified all sibling-virtualizer claims. tree.tsx:600 `estimateSize: () => virtualization?.estimateRowHeight ?? 28` AND attaches `ref={rowVirtualizer.measureElement}` at tree.tsx:1281 (dynamic measurement). virtual-branch-list.tsx:98 `estimateSize: () => virtualization.estimateSectionHeight ?? 192` AND `ref={branchVirtualizer.measureElement}` at virtual-branch-list.tsx:143. row-table.tsx has estimateSize at :421 but NO measureElement (grep: NONE) — confirmed it is the only one of the three workshop virtualizers that is purely fixed-stride. The audit's characterization is exactly as described: 01-current-state-audit.md:37-41 frames virtualization solely via 'stable item identities' — the comparator (row-table.tsx:61), fixed grid columns (:88), and CSS-grid row render (:182) — and never mentions the 96px fixed stride or absent measureElement / the height model. The omitted-context point is decisive and correct: variable-height virtualized rows are a SOLVED pattern in this exact repo. I also strengthened the finding's own caveat: a straight copy is genuinely not guaranteed — both working virtualizers additionally attach `data-index` (tree.tsx:1282, virtual-branch-list.tsx:144), which TanStack's measureElement uses to map measurements to indices, and row-table.tsx has NO data-index either. Furthermore tree/branch lists are <div>-based without a scrollMargin offset, whereas row-table renders CSS-grid <tr> elements inside a <table> with a scrollMargin correction (row-table.tsx:413,449-485,598). Porting must add measureElement + data-index AND reconcile the scrollMargin/<table> structure. High severity is appropriate (it reframes RSK7 from open research to bounded code change but with a real validation caveat).

### [MEDIUM] A2-F3 — `content-visibility:auto` + `contain-intrinsic-size:96px` on each row compounds the fixed-height overlap risk for active editors  _(type: risk)_
- **Plan loc:** 03-ux-interaction-design.md:88-98 (Layout Constraints — no mention)
- **Plan claim:** Plan does not mention content-visibility or contain-intrinsic-size at all.
- **Ground truth:** row-table.tsx:185 applies `[contain-intrinsic-size:96px] [content-visibility:auto]` to every <tr>. content-visibility:auto with a fixed intrinsic size tells the browser to reserve exactly 96px for off-screen rows and skip rendering their contents; combined with the JS fixed-stride this double-locks 96px. An express row that needs to render a taller editor while off-screen-then-scrolled-in, or that holds focus while the browser tries to apply containment, can produce layout jumps and focus/scroll instability beyond the pure-JS overlap.
- **Detail:** Even in the non-virtualized path (<75 rows), `contain-intrinsic-size:96px` provides a placeholder size for content-skipped rows. An expanding editor interacts with both the CSS containment and the JS virtualizer. The plan's truncation-in-clean-state guidance (line 95) only covers the resting state, not the active/contained interaction. This is a real but secondary hazard relative to A2-F1.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:185
- **Fix:** In U4, require either removing/overriding `content-visibility:auto`/`contain-intrinsic-size` on rows that are in active-edit, or proving via browser smoke that an expanded editor renders correctly on scroll-in. Add this to the QA browser-smoke list (currently 05:63 only checks 'no overlap' on the desktop route at default width with virtualization presumably off below 75 rows).

### [HIGH] A2-F4 — QA browser-smoke does not exercise the >=75-row virtualized case where overlap actually occurs  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 05-readiness-qa-matrix.md:63-64
- **Plan claim:** "Desktop workshop estimate route: add row, edit name/unit/cost/description, verify no overlap." / "Narrow desktop/sidebar width: long name and description truncate or wrap safely."
- **Ground truth:** Virtualization only engages at items.length >= 75 (row-table.tsx:417 `virtualEnabled = Boolean(virtualization.enabled && items.length >= (virtualization.minRows ?? 75))`). A smoke test that adds one row to a small estimate runs entirely in the NON-virtualized flow path (row-table.tsx:568 fallback branch with min-h-24 grid flow), where rows CAN grow and overlap will NOT reproduce. The exact bug surfaced by A2-F1 requires a table with >=75 rows.
- **Detail:** As written, the QA 'verify no overlap' step will almost certainly pass while the real defect ships, because a manual add-row smoke on a normal estimate stays under the 75-row virtualization threshold. The QA matrix gives false confidence on precisely the failure mode RSK7 names.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:417, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:568, src/lib/workshop/virtualization-flags.ts:39
- **Fix:** Add an explicit QA case: open/seed an estimate area with >=75 rows (or temporarily lower NEXT_PUBLIC_ESTIMATE_ROW_VIRTUALIZATION minRows), confirm `data-estimate-row-virtualizer="true"` is present, then activate an express editor on a mid-list row and verify no overlap with the next row and correct scroll height. Reference the threshold (75) and the data-attributes at row-table.tsx:561-564 as the verification hooks.
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→high) — Verified the threshold and the QA gap. Virtualization engages only at items.length >= 75: row-table.tsx:417 `virtualEnabled = Boolean(virtualization.enabled && items.length >= (virtualization.minRows ?? 75))`, with minRows default 75 from virtualization-flags.ts:39 (-> WORKSHOP_VIRTUALIZATION_DEFAULTS.estimateRowsMinRows = 75 at virtualization-flags.ts:20). The non-virtual fallback at row-table.tsx:568 maps items to `{ index, key, start: 0 }` and the per-row `style` prop is `undefined` when not virtual (row-table.tsx:594-600 ternary -> undefined), so non-virtual rows render in normal table flow at min-h-24 and CAN grow without overlap — the overlap bug from A2-F1 cannot reproduce there. The plan's Browser Smoke (05-readiness-qa-matrix.md:63 'add row ... verify no overlap', :64 narrow-width truncate/wrap) only adds a single row to an estimate and never constructs a >=75-row table, so the 'verify no overlap' step runs entirely in the non-virtual path and will pass while the real defect ships. False-confidence conclusion confirmed. Minor correction to the cited evidence: the finding pointed at virtualization-flags.ts:39 for the 75 default — line 39 is the `minRows` indirection; the literal 75 lives at virtualization-flags.ts:20 (estimateRowsMinRows). Both lines confirm the value; this does not weaken the finding. High severity stands.

### [LOW] A2-F5 — Field-name inconsistency across plan docs: unit_cost (audit/UX) vs itemCost (data model + implementation plan)  _(type: inconsistency)_
- **Plan loc:** 01-current-state-audit.md:54; 03-ux-interaction-design.md:25; 04-implementation-plan.md:81

The DB column is `unit_cost` (audit line 103 / live schema), so both names exist at different layers, but the audit/UX docs use the DB name where the client field is itemCost.
- **Detail:** Low-impact but worth normalizing: a reader building the typed patch could conflate the client `itemCost` field with a non-existent `unitCost` client field. The implementation plan U3 already gets this right (itemCost), so it's the audit/UX prose that drifts to the DB column name. Note also misc_cost vs miscCost has the same DB/client split.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/estimate-tree-data.ts:136, src/components/workshop/estimate-tree-data.ts:155, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/editable-row-model.ts:115, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/cells.tsx:99
- **Fix:** Add one line to the audit clarifying the layer mapping: client `itemCost`/`miscCost` <-> DB `unit_cost`/`misc_cost`, and use the client names when describing the editable patch and the DB names only when describing the RPC/column contract.

## Lane A3-update-contract-jsonmerge — readiness 68/100
**Summary:** The plan pack's core factual claims about the narrow update contract are accurate: the client mutation type, optimistic patch, server action input, and RPC forwarding all carry only quantity/contingencyPercentage/miscCost numeric fields, verified at use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:49/70 and actions.ts:80/2561. The plan's central KTD7 premise is CONFIRMED by reading the actual SQL: both the original (20260524033151:478-479) and operative (20260524050746:517-518) definitions of update_estimate_row_item_with_history assign metadata and source_snapshot WHOLESALE (jsonb replacement via row_patch->'metadata'), not merge (||), so partial client patches would indeed overwrite tags/detach metadata/promotion provenance. That premise is solid. However several accuracy and gap issues weaken implementation-readiness: the cited line :220 points at the wrong symbol (the per-row queue lives at 291-301, not 220); the audit understates the RPC's existing capability (it already accepts material/description/supplier/unit/discipline/designation/linkState patches, not just sourceSnapshot+metadata); the history reclassification the plan requires is a SQL change to the row_blank_added branch (20260524050746:422) that U2/U3 frame as app-only; the KTD7 server-merge approach requires widening getValidatedEstimateRow (actions.ts:691) to actually SELECT metadata/source_snapshot, which it currently does not; and naming is inconsistent (itemCost vs unitCost JSON key vs unit_cost column; "adjustment" UI label vs contingencyPercentage field) without a stated translation map. Revision-conflict and linked-row-override interactions for multi-field express edits are under-specified.

**Dimension scores:** Factual accuracy of cited code locations 72; Correctness of KTD7 JSON-merge premise 95; Completeness for an implementer (gaps) 58; Naming/terminology consistency 55

**Strengths:**
- KTD7's central premise is factually correct and load-bearing: update_estimate_row_item_with_history wholesale-replaces metadata and source_snapshot (20260524050746:517-518, and original 20260524033151:478-479), so partial client patches WOULD destroy unrelated JSON keys. The plan correctly identifies this as the gating risk.
- The merge fix KTD7 proposes is idiomatic for this codebase: the same migration already uses the || merge operator for estimate-level metadata (20260524033151:924, :1408, :1646), so adding || merge semantics to the row RPC is consistent and low-novelty.
- The narrow-contract claims are accurate end to end: UpdateEstimateRowInput (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:49-57), toOptimisticRowPatch (:70-76), UpdateEstimateRowItemInput (actions.ts:80-89), and the row_patch built at actions.ts:2564-2569 all carry only the three numeric fields — confirming the contract genuinely needs widening.
- The server-merge infrastructure largely exists: getValidatedEstimateRow already loads the row server-side (actions.ts:2562), and getSourceSnapshot/getEstimateRowMetadata helpers exist (actions.ts:739, :768), so the 'build complete merged JSON server-side' path is feasible.
- The per-row serial queue (enqueueRowUpdate, :291-301) resolves expectedRowRevision lazily inside the .then via withMutationIdentity (:296, :280), so it picks up the server-reconciled revision from the prior queued update on success — a real mitigation for cascading revision conflicts on rapid multi-field express edits.

**Findings:**

### [LOW] A3-F1 — Cited line :220 is the wrong symbol; per-row update queue is at 291-301  _(type: inaccuracy)_
- **Plan loc:** 01-current-state-audit.md:49 (Update Contract); also implied in 04-implementation-plan.md Sources :136
- **Plan claim:** 'src/hooks/use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:220 queues numeric row updates per row.'
- **Ground truth:** Line 220 is the start of `const updateMutation = useMutation({` (the mutation definition). The actual per-row queueing (rowUpdateQueuesRef, serial chaining) is enqueueRowUpdate at lines 291-301. The optimistic numeric patch is at 70-76 and 222-245.
- **Detail:** The behavior described (per-row queued numeric updates) is real, but the line points at the mutation factory, not the queue. An implementer following :220 to find the queue mechanism would land on the wrong code.
- **Evidence:** src/hooks/use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:220, src/hooks/use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:291-301
- **Fix:** Repoint the citation: 'updateMutation defined at :220; per-row serialization in enqueueRowUpdate at :291-301 (queue map rowUpdateQueuesRef at :135).'

### [INFO] A3-F2 — KTD7 premise confirmed: row RPC replaces (not merges) metadata and source_snapshot  _(type: strength)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-implementation-plan.md:39 (KTD7); 02-data-model-and-supabase-research.md:116
- **Plan claim:** KTD7: 'Because the current RPC replaces metadata and source_snapshot, express updates must build complete merged JSON server-side or add RPC-level merge semantics.' And 02-...:116 'Do not let partial client patches overwrite tags, detach metadata, promotion provenance, or source evidence.'
- **Ground truth:** Operative function 20260524050746:517 `source_snapshot = case when row_patch ? 'sourceSnapshot' then row_patch->'sourceSnapshot' else row_item.source_snapshot end` and :518 `metadata = case when row_patch ? 'metadata' then coalesce(row_patch->'metadata', '{}'::jsonb) else row_item.metadata end`. Both are wholesale jsonb assignment, not ||. Identical in original 20260524033151:478-479. Note coalesce(...,'{}') means a present-but-null metadata patch RESETS metadata to empty object.
- **Detail:** Verified against the actual SQL of both definitions of update_estimate_row_item_with_history. A partial client patch of metadata or source_snapshot replaces the entire JSONB column, destroying any keys not in the patch (e.g. metadata.expressComponent, detach provenance, tags). The plan's premise is correct and is the right gate to flag.
- **Evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260524050746_add_estimate_row_contingency_percentage.sql:517-518, supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:478-479
- **Fix:** Keep KTD7 but add the concrete worst case to the doc: 'a patch of {metadata:{expressComponent:...}} on a detached library row erases prior detach/source provenance; a null metadata coalesces to {}.' Prefer the RPC-level || merge option since the codebase already merges estimate metadata with || (20260524033151:924).

### [HIGH] A3-F3 — KTD7 server-merge path needs getValidatedEstimateRow widened to SELECT metadata/source_snapshot  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-implementation-plan.md:39 (KTD7), :81 (U3 Details)
- **Plan claim:** KTD7 / U3: 'the server action reads the current row and writes complete merged metadata and source_snapshot.'
- **Ground truth:** The update action's row loader getValidatedEstimateRow (actions.ts:691-697) selects only `id, node_id, estimate_id, source_library_item_id, link_state, row_revision` plus the node join. It does NOT select `metadata` or `source_snapshot`. The 'read current row and merge' approach therefore cannot work without changing this SELECT (or doing a second fetch).
- **Detail:** The plan presents server-side merge as if the current row is already available to merge against. It is not — the existing validated-row query deliberately omits the JSON columns. This is a concrete, load-bearing change U3 does not list.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:691-697, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2562
- **Fix:** In U3, add a task: widen getValidatedEstimateRow's SELECT to include metadata and source_snapshot (or add a dedicated loader) so the server can compute merged JSON. Alternatively choose the RPC-|| approach (A3-F2) and skip the app-side read-merge entirely; the doc should pick one path explicitly.
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→high) — Independently confirmed against the actual code. getValidatedEstimateRow's SELECT (actions.ts:693) is exactly `id, node_id, estimate_id, source_library_item_id, link_state, row_revision, estimate_nodes!inner(id, kind, estimates!inner(project_id))` — it does NOT select `metadata` or `source_snapshot`. updateEstimateRowItem destructures `{ project, supabase, row }` from this loader (actions.ts:2562) and uses `row.source_library_item_id` / `row.row_revision` (2568, 2575), so the loaded `row` object genuinely lacks the JSON columns. The plan's U3 detail (04-implementation-plan.md:81) says 'the server action reads the current row and writes complete merged metadata and source_snapshot' — quoted accurately by the auditor — but a server-side merge against the current row is impossible without either widening this SELECT or doing a second fetch. The auditor's evidence line numbers (691-697, 2562) are exact. One small fairness note that does NOT change the verdict: U3's Files line (04-implementation-plan.md:80) DOES list `src/lib/estimates/actions.ts`, so editing the loader is technically within the touched-file scope; the gap is that the plan never names the specific SELECT-widening (or second-fetch) step and presents the row as already mergeable. That is still a real, load-bearing, unstated change. High severity is appropriate: if implemented literally, the merge silently reads `undefined` for metadata/source_snapshot and could clobber those columns. Slightly understated by the auditor only in that the alternative 'or the RPC accepts explicit merge patches' branch (line 81) would avoid the loader change entirely — so the SELECT-widening is required only for the server-action-merge route, not unconditionally. The plan offering that RPC-merge alternative is a partial mitigation the finding omits, but the named/default route (server action reads current row) is in fact broken as written.

### [MEDIUM] A3-F4 — actions.ts:2561 forwards linkState too (linked-overridden), not 'only numeric fields'  _(type: inaccuracy)_
- **Plan loc:** 01-current-state-audit.md:51 (Update Contract)
- **Plan claim:** 'src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2561 forwards only numeric row fields into update_estimate_row_item_with_history.'
- **Ground truth:** The row_patch built at actions.ts:2564-2569 includes a fourth conditional field: `...(row.source_library_item_id ? { linkState: 'linked-overridden' } : {})`. So any update to a LINKED row also forwards linkState='linked-overridden'. It is not purely numeric.
- **Detail:** This is the existing linked-row override behavior — relevant to the task's 'linked-row override' gap. The audit's 'only numeric' framing hides a side effect U3 must preserve: editing a linked library row flips it to linked-overridden. When U3 widens the patch to material/description/etc., an implementer who copies this branch will (correctly) override linked rows on any field edit, but the plan never states this is intended for express widening.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2564-2569
- **Fix:** Correct the audit to: 'forwards quantity/contingencyPercentage/miscCost plus an automatic linkState=linked-overridden when the row has a source_library_item_id (actions.ts:2568).' In U3, state explicitly whether widened express/material edits on linked rows should keep this auto-override (likely yes) and confirm express/detached rows (source_library_item_id null) correctly skip it.

### [MEDIUM] A3-F5 — Audit understates the RPC: it already accepts material/description/supplier/unit/discipline/designation/linkState/categoryNodeId/supplierId patches  _(type: inaccuracy)_
- **Plan loc:** 01-current-state-audit.md:52; 02-...:116
- **Plan claim:** 'migration 20260524033151:478 already supports sourceSnapshot and metadata in the underlying row patch, so the RPC can carry richer row edits if the app action widens the contract safely.'
- **Ground truth:** The operative update RPC's UPDATE statement already has CASE branches for quantity, unitCost, contingencyPercentage, miscCost, material, description, supplier, unit, discipline, designation, linkState, categoryNodeId, supplierId, sourceLibraryItemId, sourceSnapshot, and metadata (20260524050746:503-518). The RPC is NOT limited to sourceSnapshot+metadata.
- **Detail:** The audit names only sourceSnapshot and metadata, implying those are the extent of the RPC's headroom. In reality the RPC already persists every express field U3 wants (material/description/supplier/unit/unitCost) without any SQL change. This materially changes U3's scope: the migration-change risk it hedges ('supabase/migrations/* only if RPC payload validation needs a compatibility change') is unnecessary for the text/cost fields — only the JSON-merge decision (A3-F2) and history reclassification (A3-F6) touch SQL.
- **Evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260524050746_add_estimate_row_contingency_percentage.sql:503-518, supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:466-479
- **Fix:** Rewrite the audit line to enumerate the full set of already-supported row_patch keys, and update U3 to make clear the RPC needs NO change for material/description/supplier/unit/unitCost — the work is purely app-action + hook + tree-patch widening, plus the JSON-merge decision.

### [HIGH] A3-F6 — History reclassification of express vs blank requires a SQL change to the row_blank_added branch, not app-only  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-implementation-plan.md:74 (U2 History contract), :52 (02 doc), :80 (U3 Files hedge)
- **Plan claim:** U2 History contract: 'The insert history path must classify only legacy blank placeholders as row_blank_added. Express inserts use the existing row_added activity.' Framed alongside 'migrations only if RPC payload validation needs a compatibility change.'
- **Ground truth:** The operative insert RPC hardcodes classification at 20260524050746:422 `case when inserted_row.source_library_item_id is null then 'row_blank_added' else 'row_added' end` (also 20260524033151:384). Express rows have source_library_item_id = null, so they will be classified row_blank_added by this SQL. Narrowing this to 'legacy blank only' requires editing the RPC (e.g. inspect metadata.expressComponent), which is a migration, not app code.
- **Detail:** The plan asserts the desired classification but never acknowledges it is a server-side SQL branch. There is no app-layer hook to reclassify; the event_type is decided inside insert_estimate_row_item_with_history. This is a real migration the implementation plan omits from U2's file list (U2 lists no supabase/migrations files at all).
- **Evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260524050746_add_estimate_row_contingency_percentage.sql:422, supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:384
- **Fix:** Add an explicit migration task under U2: update the insert RPC's event_type CASE to treat express rows (source_library_item_id null AND metadata.expressComponent.kind='express') as row_added (or a new event), keeping row_blank_added only for true legacy placeholders. List supabase/migrations/* in U2's Files. Decide whether to backfill/relabel existing express inserts.
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→high) — Independently confirmed. The event_type classification is hardcoded in SQL: `case when inserted_row.source_library_item_id is null then 'row_blank_added' else 'row_added' end` at 20260524050746:422 AND 20260524033151:384 — both line numbers exact. I verified 20260524050746 is the OPERATIVE definition: a repo-wide search shows only these two migrations define insert_estimate_row_item_with_history or reference row_blank_added, and 050746 has the later timestamp (no migration after it redefines the function or changes the branch). Express rows are inserted with source_library_item_id = null (the plan's KTD2 keeps express identity in metadata, not a library link), so they WILL be classified row_blank_added by this SQL — directly contradicting U2's history contract (04-implementation-plan.md:74) that express inserts 'use the existing row_added activity.' The reclassification cannot happen in app code: the literal 'row_blank_added'/'row_added' event_type is emitted only inside the RPC (it is written into estimate_sheet_events at 050746:417-432); app code in activity-history.tsx / activity-composer.ts only reads, labels, and filters event types, never assigns them at insert. The auditor's suggested fix (inspect metadata.expressComponent in SQL) is feasible because metadata IS persisted into the row at insert (050746:397, `coalesce(row_payload->'metadata','{}'::jsonb)`), so inserted_row.metadata is in scope at line 422 — but `expressComponent` does not yet exist anywhere in src/lib/estimates (it is a proposed KTD2/U1 metadata key, 04-implementation-plan.md:34,65), confirming this is net-new RPC logic = a migration. U2's Files line (04-implementation-plan.md:71) lists only EstimateSheetContent.tsx + two test files and NO supabase/migrations — confirming the omission. One correction to the auditor's framing (does not change verdict, and actually strengthens the finding): the phrase 'migrations only if RPC payload validation needs a compatibility change' is on line 80, which is U3's Files line, NOT U2's. The auditor implied it sits alongside U2; it does not. U2 has no migration caveat at all, so the gap is even cleaner than stated. High severity holds.

### [MEDIUM] A3-F7 — Field-name inconsistency across layers (itemCost vs unitCost JSON key vs unit_cost column) is unstated  _(type: inconsistency)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-implementation-plan.md:81 (U3 Details)
- **Plan claim:** U3: 'Add typed patch fields for material, description, supplier, unit, itemCost, miscCost, sourceSnapshot, and metadata.'
- **Ground truth:** Three distinct names for unit cost across the stack: domain/app field = itemCost (estimate-row-tree-patch.ts:5, UpdateEstimateRowItemResult.itemCost at actions.ts:104); RPC row_patch JSON key = unitCost (SQL `row_patch ? 'unitCost'` at 20260524050746:504; insert payload `unitCost: libraryItem.itemCost` at actions.ts:2546/2606); DB column = unit_cost. The result reader maps row.unitCost -> itemCost at actions.ts:351.
- **Detail:** U3 says to add an itemCost patch field, which is correct at the domain layer, but the implementer wiring the patch into the row_patch JSON must translate itemCost -> 'unitCost' or the RPC silently ignores it (the CASE only fires on `row_patch ? 'unitCost'`). The plan never states this translation, and EstimateRowItemPatch (estimate-row-tree-patch.ts:3-12) uses itemCost while the RPC uses unitCost — a classic silent-no-op trap.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/estimates/estimate-row-tree-patch.ts:5, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:351, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2546, supabase/migrations/20260524050746_add_estimate_row_contingency_percentage.sql:504
- **Fix:** Add a name-mapping note to U3: domain itemCost <-> row_patch key 'unitCost' <-> column unit_cost. Specify that the widened action must emit 'unitCost' (not 'itemCost') into row_patch, mirroring the insert/replace payloads at actions.ts:2546/2606.

### [LOW] A3-F8 — 'adjustment' (audit/UI label) vs 'contingencyPercentage' (contract) used interchangeably without mapping  _(type: inconsistency)_
- **Plan loc:** 01-current-state-audit.md:35; 04-implementation-plan.md:47 (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:49)
- **Plan claim:** Audit: row table edits 'quantity, adjustment, and misc cost only.' Contract/U3 use contingencyPercentage.
- **Ground truth:** The middle editable column's user-facing label is 'adjustment' (row-table.tsx:275 'adjustment type', :314 'adjustment percentage'), but the underlying field is contingencyPercentage/contingencyMode (row-table.tsx:280-284, 296-307). contingencyMode is resolved client-side to a single contingencyPercentage before updateRow (EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1694); the server contract only ever sees contingencyPercentage.
- **Detail:** The plan uses 'adjustment' (audit) and 'contingencyPercentage' (contract/U3) for the same column without stating they are the same thing, and never mentions contingencyMode (a UI-only concept that does not cross the mutation boundary). Minor, but an implementer could mistake 'adjustment' for a separate field to widen.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:275, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:314, src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1694
- **Fix:** Add a one-line glossary: 'adjustment' (UI label) = contingencyPercentage (field); contingencyMode is UI-only and is collapsed into contingencyPercentage before commit. Confirm express editing does not need to touch adjustment/contingency at all in the first pass.

### [MEDIUM] A3-F9 — Revision-conflict handling for multi-field express edits is under-specified  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-implementation-plan.md:82 (U3 Tests), :39 (KTD7)
- **Plan claim:** R3/U3 test: 'stale revision rolls back with failed state.' KTD7 says preserve 'expected revision checks.'
- **Ground truth:** The RPC raises estimate_sheet_row_revision_conflict (P0001) on mismatch (20260524050746:489-497). The client has NO special handling for this code: updateMutation.onError (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:257-267) just restores previousPatch and markFailed(), then invalidateQueries with refetchType:'none' (no refetch). A grep found zero references to the conflict code anywhere in src/.
- **Detail:** For a single numeric field the current UX (silent rollback + failed flag, stale cached value, no auto-refetch) is tolerable. For express rows that commit material+unitCost+metadata together, a stale revision fails the WHOLE patch, the user loses all entered fields with only a generic failed state, and refetchType:'none' leaves the rolled-back (possibly wrong) value on screen. The plan never defines conflict-recovery UX for the widened multi-field case.
- **Evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260524050746_add_estimate_row_contingency_percentage.sql:489-497, src/hooks/use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:257-267
- **Fix:** Specify express-row conflict UX in U3/R3: on P0001, refetch the row (or use refetchType:'active'), surface a 'someone else changed this row' message, and preserve the user's unsaved field draft so a single retry can re-apply against the fresh revision. Add a test for multi-field patch losing data on conflict.

### [MEDIUM] A3-F10 — Linked-row override interaction with widened express/material edits not analyzed  _(type: risk)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-implementation-plan.md:81 (U3), :37 (KTD5)
- **Plan claim:** U3: 'Preserve expected revision checks and linked row override behavior.' KTD5/R4 cover icon eligibility but not edit semantics.
- **Ground truth:** actions.ts:2568 auto-sets linkState='linked-overridden' for any update to a row with source_library_item_id. The update RPC also accepts a direct linkState patch (20260524050746:513). If U3's widened action both auto-derives linked-overridden AND lets the client send linkState (e.g. via the EstimateRowItemPatch which already has linkState), the two could conflict.
- **Detail:** U3 says 'preserve linked row override behavior' but does not say HOW the widened multi-field path interacts with it: (a) Should editing material on a linked library row flip it to linked-overridden (currently yes)? (b) Should express rows (unlinked) ever be sent a linkState patch? (c) The RPC will happily apply a client-sent linkState (SQL :513), bypassing the server-derived branch — a correctness/authority question the plan leaves open.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2568, supabase/migrations/20260524050746_add_estimate_row_contingency_percentage.sql:513, src/lib/estimates/estimate-row-tree-patch.ts:11
- **Fix:** In U3 define the linkState authority rule: server derives linkState (keep the :2568 branch), client patch never sets linkState directly for express/material edits. State that express rows skip the override branch because source_library_item_id is null, and add a test asserting a material edit on a linked row produces linked-overridden while the same edit on an express row does not change link_state.

## Lane A4-project-specific-icon-bug — readiness 62/100
**Summary:** All five line-anchored claims in Lane A4 are essentially correct at commit 72628d46: cells.tsx:75-81 gate the Cube action, use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:171 optimistically detaches, and EstimateSheetContent.tsx:2068 gates the confirmation flow behind canMakeProjectSpecific (with a small precision error: 2068 only opens the dialog state; the actual detach runs in confirmProjectSpecific at 2074). The audit correctly identifies that eligibility is field-derived (sourceLibraryItemId OR a linked/source-changed linkState), not index/duplicate-derived. The plan's strongest conclusion (KTD5/U5: eligibility comes from persisted link fields) is directionally right. HOWEVER the audit's hypothesis list is speculative and internally inconsistent with the verified code: the optimistic clone (cloneLibraryItemForEstimate, EstimateSheetContent.tsx:352) preserves the dragged item's fields verbatim, and real DB-backed library components already carry sourceLibraryItemId (component-library-repository.ts:165), so both first and second instances should pass the gate immediately — the 'only second instance' symptom is NOT explained by any code path. The single field that determines server link state is getSourceLibraryItemId (actions.ts:782) feeding linkState: sourceLibraryItemId ? 'linked-clean' : 'unlinked' (actions.ts:2552); rows fail the gate only when that id is absent/non-UUID, which is exactly what the seed/test fixtures (estimateLibraryItems) hit. The plan should reproduce the bug to pin the mechanism or reframe U5 as a robustness fix.

**Dimension scores:** Claim accuracy (cited lines/behaviors) 78; Root-cause correctness 45; Fix adequacy (U5) 60; Test coverage of the diagnosis 55; Internal consistency 65

**Strengths:**
- The core technical claim is correct and verified: eligibility for the Cube action is derived from row source/link fields (canMakeProjectSpecific, cells.tsx:79-82), not from row index or duplicate count.
- The audit correctly located the real server-side determinant of link state: linkState is set to 'linked-clean' only when a source library id is present (actions.ts:2552), with getSourceLibraryItemId (actions.ts:782-785) as the true arbiter.
- All cited line numbers are accurate or within 1-2 lines, and the detach optimistic patch (source:'project', sourceLibraryItemId:null, linkState:'detached') matches use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:190-195 exactly.
- U5's proposed normalization ('library rows arrive without link state but have a source id', 04-implementation-plan.md:96) is a legitimate defensive fix for the one genuinely reproducible failure mode.

**Findings:**

### [HIGH] A4-F1 — Bug hypothesis list is unverified speculation; true 'first vs second instance' mechanism is not established by any code path  _(type: missing-evidence)_
- **Plan loc:** 01-current-state-audit.md, 'Project-Specific Icon Bug Surface' (lines 66-73)
- **Plan claim:** Four 'likely failure modes' for the 'icon only appears on the second instance' bug, none reproduced.
- **Ground truth:** Optimistic clone preserves all link fields; DB-backed components already carry sourceLibraryItemId; insert does not refetch (refetchType:'none'); both instances are structurally identical and should pass the gate immediately.
- **Detail:** The audit lists four 'likely failure modes' (01-current-state-audit.md:66-73) for 'icon only appears on the second instance', e.g. 'first inserted component lacks sourceLibraryItemId or a linked linkState in the client payload' and 'optimistic insert uses a cloned row locally but passes the un-cloned libraryItem to the action'. Ground truth: cloneLibraryItemForEstimate (EstimateSheetContent.tsx:352-369) spreads the full libraryItem (...libraryItem) and changes only id/quantity/totals; it preserves source, sourceLibraryItemId, and linkState. Real DB-backed library components already carry sourceLibraryItemId: row.id (component-library-repository.ts:165), so the optimistic cloned row passes canMakeProjectSpecific via the item.sourceLibraryItemId branch (cells.tsx:81) immediately, regardless of index. The insert mutation invalidates with refetchType:'none' (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:144), so no refetch replaces the optimistic row. There is no code path where the first clone differs from the second clone of the same item. The 'passes the un-cloned libraryItem to the action' hypothesis is literally true (insertLibraryItem passes libraryItem, EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1727) but harmless: the server re-derives link fields from that same libraryItem, and the optimistic row already carries them.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:352-369, src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1706, src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1727, src/hooks/use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:144, src/lib/repositories/component-library-repository.ts:165, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/cells.tsx:79-82
- **Fix:** Reproduce the bug against a REAL project (not seed fixtures) and capture the first vs second instance's source_library_item_id / link_state before claiming a root cause. If it cannot be reproduced with DB-backed components, downgrade the 'second instance' framing to 'unconfirmed report' and reframe U5 as a robustness fix rather than a confirmed root-cause fix.
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→high) — Re-checking confirms the original auditor's core thesis: the four 'likely failure modes' at 01-current-state-audit.md:66-73 are unverified speculation, and there is no code path where a first clone differs structurally from a second clone of the same item. Evidence verified line-by-line: (1) cloneLibraryItemForEstimate (EstimateSheetContent.tsx:352-369) spreads ...libraryItem and only overrides id/quantity/subTotal/contingency/misc/totals — it does NOT touch source, sourceLibraryItemId, or linkState, so any link fields present on the input survive to BOTH clones identically. (2) The insert/detach mutations all invalidate with refetchType:'none' (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:144,145,150,205,206,216,254,255,266) — confirmed there is NO refetch path that replaces the optimistic row with server data in-session, so the server's linkState write cannot retroactively change a first vs second instance. (3) DB-backed library components carry sourceLibraryItemId: row.id (component-library-repository.ts:165), where row.id is a real UUID, so canMakeProjectSpecific (cells.tsx:79-81) returns true via the sourceLibraryItemId branch immediately and identically for every instance. (4) insertLibraryItem passes the un-cloned libraryItem to insertRow (EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1727) — literally true but harmless, since the server re-derives sourceLibraryItemId/linkState from that same libraryItem (actions.ts:2537,2552) and the never-refetched optimistic row already carries the fields. ONE CORRECTION/AMPLIFICATION the original A4-F1 glosses over: its claim that 'both instances should pass the gate immediately regardless of index' holds for REAL DB-backed components but NOT for seed/demo items. For a seed clone (e.g. lib-sofa, estimate-tree-data.ts:155) sourceLibraryItemId and linkState are both undefined, so canMakeProjectSpecific returns Boolean(undefined || undefined?.startsWith(...) || ...) === FALSE for BOTH instances — they uniformly FAIL the gate, not pass it. Either way the conclusion stands and is strengthened: instance order is irrelevant; the 'second instance only' framing is unsupported by any code path. Severity high is appropriate — the plan's bug section rests on speculation rather than a reproduced mechanism.

### [HIGH] A4-F2 — The one reproducible gate failure is the SEED/test library data, not 'the first instance' — plan misses this  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-implementation-plan.md, U5 (lines 92-97)
- **Plan claim:** U5 frames the bug as duplicate/second-instance specific and proposes preserving link fields through clone+reconcile.
- **Ground truth:** Rows with non-UUID ids or missing source_library_item_id fail the gate permanently and independently of instance order; this is the only code-grounded failure mode and is exercised by the seed fixtures.
- **Detail:** U5 aims to 'Show the project-specific action for every eligible linked component instance' (04-implementation-plan.md:92-97). Ground truth: the seed library items in estimateLibraryItems carry source:'library' but have NON-UUID ids (e.g. 'lib-sofa', estimate-tree-data.ts:155) and NO sourceLibraryItemId (fields optional, types.ts:112-113). getSourceLibraryItemId returns null for them because the id is not a UUID and sourceLibraryItemId is absent (actions.ts:782-785), so the server writes linkState:'unlinked' (actions.ts:2552) and the gate fails PERMANENTLY for every such row — first and second instance alike. The existing library-insert test even inserts estimateLibraryItems[0] (tests/vitest/estimate-sheet-library-insert.test.tsx:233). This is the only failure mode grounded in code, and it is index-independent, contradicting the 'second instance only' narrative.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/estimate-tree-data.ts:155, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:782-785, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2552, src/lib/estimates/types.ts:112-113, tests/vitest/estimate-sheet-library-insert.test.tsx:233
- **Fix:** State explicitly in U5 that components with non-UUID ids or a missing source_library_item_id are the rows that fail the gate, independent of order. If the reporter saw the icon on a 'second instance', verify whether the two instances came from different sources (seed/manual vs DB component) rather than assuming a duplicate-count bug.
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→high) — Confirmed with one important mechanism correction. The seed library items do carry source:'library' with NON-UUID ids and NO link fields — verified directly: lib-sofa is row({ id: 'lib-sofa', ..., source: 'library', ... }) at estimate-tree-data.ts:155, the row() helper only spreads ...input and never injects sourceLibraryItemId or linkState (estimate-tree-data.ts:135-152), and rg confirms ZERO occurrences of sourceLibraryItemId/linkState anywhere in estimate-tree-data.ts. isUuid uses /^[0-9a-f]{8}-.../ (ids.ts:1-5), so 'lib-sofa' is not a UUID. getSourceLibraryItemId (actions.ts:782-785) therefore returns null for seed items (sourceLibraryItemId absent AND id not a UUID), and the server writes linkState: sourceLibraryItemId ? 'linked-clean' : 'unlinked' === 'unlinked' (actions.ts:2552). sourceLibraryItemId and linkState are optional on EstimateRowItem (types.ts:112-113). The existing test inserts estimateLibraryItems[0] === lib-sofa (estimate-sheet-library-insert.test.tsx:234, dispatched at :237; note the cited line :233 is actually the renderPersistedEstimateSheet() call — minor off-by-one, the seed insert is :234/:237). This IS a real, index-independent, code-grounded failure mode that U5 (04-implementation-plan.md:92-97) does not name. CORRECTION the original A4-F2 overstates the server's role: because every insert/detach uses refetchType:'none' (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:144), the server's linkState:'unlinked' write is NEVER read back in-session — the gate actually fails CLIENT-SIDE on the optimistic clone, since the seed clone has undefined sourceLibraryItemId AND undefined linkState, making canMakeProjectSpecific (cells.tsx:81) return false directly. The server write only becomes the failure mechanism on a fresh page load / cold fetch. So A4-F2's conclusion ('only code-grounded failure mode, index-independent, contradicts second-instance narrative') is correct and well-supported, but the precise mechanism is client-gate-on-undefined-fields in-session, with the server unlinked-write as the persistence-layer reinforcement — not purely 'the server writes unlinked so the gate fails' as phrased. Severity high stands: this is the only reproducible gate failure and the plan's U5 framing/tests ('two same-library instances both show the Cube action') would not actually exercise or fix it for seed data.

### [LOW] A4-F3 — Plan claims EstimateSheetContent.tsx:2068 opens the confirmation dialog; 2068 only stores the row id — detach runs in confirmProjectSpecific (2074)  _(type: inaccuracy)_
- **Plan loc:** 01-current-state-audit.md:64 (also U5 at 04-implementation-plan.md:95)
- **Plan claim:** 'src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:2068 opens a confirmation dialog only after canMakeProjectSpecific(rowItem) passes.'

Ground truth: at line 2068, requestProjectSpecific guards with canMakeProjectSpecific (2070) and then only calls setProjectSpecificRowId(rowItemId) (2071) — it stores the id. The dialog is rendered off that state elsewhere, and the actual detach mutation fires in confirmProjectSpecific at 2074-2089 via detachRow. The gate is described correctly; the 'opens a confirmation dialog' attribution to 2068 is imprecise.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:2068-2072, src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:2074-2089
- **Fix:** Correct the citation to: 'requestProjectSpecific (2068) gates on canMakeProjectSpecific then sets projectSpecificRowId; confirmProjectSpecific (2074) runs the detach.' U5 should target both functions.

The detach onMutate/onSuccess/onError all use this same expression (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:175, 200, 209), and the hook computes treeQueryKey the same way (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:137). There is no divergence in key derivation for tabbed vs non-tabbed estimates within this hook.
