## Lane A7-promotion-component-library — readiness 52/100
**Summary:** The plan's factual claims about the existing component-library surface are accurate: src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:431 is exactly createCostLibraryComponent, it stamps metadata provenance (source/isCustom/createdFrom), and it creates BOTH the cost_library_items row and its price option(s) atomically inside one SECURITY DEFINER plpgsql RPC (create_cost_library_component). The price-option table genuinely stores unit/unit_cost/misc_cost/pricing_source/confidence and image columns, so claim 2 in the data-model doc holds. The dangerous gap is atomicity/idempotency of the END-TO-END promotion: there is NO existing transaction boundary that creates a library item AND relinks the source estimate_row_items row. Library create is a workspace-scoped RPC; the relink (update_estimate_row_item_with_history) is a separate project-scoped RPC — two round-trips, two Postgres transactions, no shared boundary. The library-create RPC has no correlation_id/upsert idempotency, the row-update RPC does have correlation idempotency but only within the estimate-sheet event log and enforces expected_row_revision (a real relink-conflict failure mode), and the plan's chosen guard (metadata.expressComponent.promotedLibraryItemId) is written only in the relink step, so a create-succeeds/relink-fails crash orphans a library component with no guard set — the exact partial-failure the plan claims to prevent. The plan's "prefer one RPC, otherwise idempotent retry lookup before create" hedge is technically buildable but is NOT honest that the safe path realistically requires a NEW migration/RPC, which contradicts its own "no migration by default" / R9 posture.

**Dimension scores:** Claim accuracy (cited lines/behaviors) 88; Atomicity / idempotency design soundness 38; Honesty about needing a new migration/RPC 45; Partial-failure / concurrency / conflict coverage 35; Reuse-surface correctness 80

**Strengths:**
- actions.ts:431 line/symbol/behavior claim is exactly correct — createCostLibraryComponent stamps provenance and creates item + price options atomically in one SECURITY DEFINER plpgsql RPC.
- The price-option schema and create RPC genuinely store unit/unit_cost/misc_cost/pricing_source/confidence and image (image_bucket/image_path/thumbnail_path/image_metadata), fully backing the data-model's storage claim.
- The plan correctly identifies the JSON-replace hazard (KTD7) and the link-state enum already has the needed states (linked-clean, detached) so no enum migration is needed for U7's target state — verified against the core schema enum.
- createCostLibraryComponent is a real, already-used reuse surface (ComponentLibraryManager, AddComponentModal), so KTD4's 'existing create/update RPC surface' premise is sound.
- The relink RPC (update_estimate_row_item_with_history) genuinely supports sourceLibraryItemId/sourceSnapshot/linkState/metadata in its patch, so the row half of promotion needs no new RPC at the SQL level.

**Findings:**

### [INFO] A7-F1 — actions.ts:431 claim is accurate — createCostLibraryComponent creates item + price options with provenance, atomically  _(type: strength)_
- **Plan loc:** 02-data-model-and-supabase-research.md 'Conversion Paths' / 04-implementation-plan.md KTD4, U7
- **Plan claim:** actions.ts:431 creates a cost_library_items component with metadata provenance; promotion creates the library item and price option.
- **Ground truth:** Confirmed at exact line 431; metadata provenance present; item + price options created together in one atomic plpgsql RPC.
- **Detail:** The data-model doc (Conversion Paths) and KTD4 cite src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:431 as creating a cost_library_items component with metadata provenance. Verified exact: line 431 is `export async function createCostLibraryComponent(input: CreateCostLibraryComponentInput)`. It stamps metadata provenance at actions.ts:438-443 (source:"library", isCustom:true, createdFrom:"workshop-library"), normalizes price options (actions.ts:445), and calls the RPC `create_cost_library_component` with both component and price_options (actions.ts:448-452). The underlying RPC (supabase/migrations/20260524141347_...:131) is a single plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER function that INSERTs the cost_library_items row (lines 203-254) AND the cost_library_item_price_options rows (lines 256-319) in one transaction. So the item+priceOption pair IS created atomically. This is a genuine strength: KTD4's 'creates the default price option' is correct, and it is transactional for the create half.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:431, src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:438-452, supabase/migrations/20260524141347_component_library_identity_variant_sku_brand_view.sql:131-319
- **Fix:** Keep the claim. Note in U7 that createCostLibraryComponent already requires priceOptions to be non-empty with exactly one default (validatePriceOptions, actions.ts:228-231), so the promotion wrapper MUST synthesize a default price option from the express row's unit/unit_cost/misc_cost or the create call throws 'At least one variant is required.'

### [INFO] A7-F2 — Price-option table genuinely stores unit/unit_cost/misc_cost/source/confidence/image — claim 2 verified  _(type: strength)_
- **Plan loc:** 02-data-model-and-supabase-research.md 'Conversion Paths' (Express To Library-Wide)
- **Plan claim:** Existing price option RPCs can store unit, unit cost, misc cost, source, confidence, and optional image data.
- **Ground truth:** Table and create RPC store all listed fields including image_bucket/image_path/thumbnail_path/image_metadata.
- **Detail:** Data-model doc states 'Existing price option RPCs can store unit, unit cost, misc cost, source, confidence, and optional image data.' Verified against the table DDL: cost_library_item_price_options has unit (text), unit_cost (numeric), misc_cost (numeric), pricing_source (text = 'source'), confidence (text), plus source_url. Image storage is present in the RPC insert column list: image_bucket, image_path, thumbnail_path, image_metadata (migration 20260524141347:279-282), mapped from option JSON keys imageBucket/imagePath/thumbnailPath (lines 311-314). So every field the plan enumerates is storable.
- **Evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260523120957_component_library_item_versioning.sql:40-60, supabase/migrations/20260524141347_component_library_identity_variant_sku_brand_view.sql:256-319
- **Fix:** Accurate. If express image is stored as a plain URL in source_snapshot (per the doc's deferral of file upload), clarify that promotion maps that URL into the price option's source_url / image_path, since the price-option schema separates source_url (a string) from image_bucket/image_path (storage refs).

### [CRITICAL] A7-F3 — CRITICAL: No existing transaction boundary spans library-create + row-relink; promotion is two separate Postgres transactions  _(type: risk)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-implementation-plan.md U7 + R9 + 02-...md 'Conversion Paths' last bullet
- **Plan claim:** Prefer one Postgres RPC/transaction for the create-plus-row-update boundary; otherwise implement idempotent retry lookup before create. (no migration by default / R9)
- **Ground truth:** No RPC today spans workspace-library and project-estimate tables. The two-call action path is two transactions with no atomic boundary.
- **Detail:** The plan says 'prefer one Postgres RPC/transaction for the create-plus-row-update boundary' (U7) but the codebase has no such boundary, and the two halves live in different domains so no current RPC can do both. create_cost_library_component is workspace-scoped (target_workspace_id; SECURITY DEFINER over cost_library_* tables). The relink half must use update_estimate_row_item_with_history, which is project/estimate-scoped (target_project_id, target_estimate_id; resolve_estimate_workspace; row-level locks on estimates/estimate_row_items). These are invoked as two separate supabase.rpc() calls = two independent transactions/round-trips. The action layer does NOT wrap them (each action awaits its own createSupabaseServerClient and rpc). PostgREST gives no client-side multi-RPC transaction. Therefore a create-succeeds-then-relink-fails crash (network drop, revision conflict, process kill) leaves an ORPHANED library component plus an un-relinked express row. The plan presents the single-transaction option as available 'by default'; it is not — it requires a new plpgsql RPC that touches both cost_library_items and estimate_row_items, i.e. a new migration.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:448-452, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2571-2580, supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:405-432, supabase/migrations/20260524141347_component_library_identity_variant_sku_brand_view.sql:131-140
- **Fix:** State plainly in U7 that the truly-atomic path REQUIRES a new SECURITY DEFINER RPC (e.g. promote_express_row_to_library_component) that, in one transaction, inserts the library item+price option and updates the estimate_row_items row, plus matching grants/RLS-definer audit — and that this contradicts the 'no migration by default' posture. Make the migration an explicit, recommended scope item for U7 rather than a fallback, OR fully own the two-transaction saga risk (see A7-F4/F5).
- **Adversarial verify:** `overstated` (sev→high) — The core technical claim is CONFIRMED: no existing RPC spans both domains. I scanned every migration for a function body that both `insert into public.cost_library_items` and touches `estimate_row_items` and found NONE (the only both-table files are schema/seed/RLS files, not a combined function; `unlink_estimate_row_item_from_library_with_history` is the inverse and never inserts into cost_library_items). create_cost_library_component takes only (target_workspace_id, component, price_options) and has 0 references to estimate_row_items in its body. The action layer makes two independent supabase.rpc() calls: createCostLibraryComponent at src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:448-452 (its own createSupabaseServerClient at :435) and update_estimate_row_item_with_history at src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2571-2580 (its own validated client). PostgREST cannot wrap two RPC calls in one transaction, so a create-succeeds/relink-fails window genuinely orphans a library item. HOWEVER the finding OVERSTATES one point: it claims 'the plan presents the single-transaction option as available by default; it is not.' The plan does NOT claim the transaction RPC exists today. Data-model line 63 and impl-plan U7 line 110 both say 'PREFER one Postgres RPC/transaction... OTHERWISE implement idempotent retry lookup before create' — conditional language. And the QA matrix at 05-readiness-qa-matrix.md:73 explicitly lists 'add an atomic/idempotent promotion RPC' as a possible migration, acknowledging it is not free. The real (valid) gap is narrower: U7's Files list (04-implementation-plan.md:109) omits supabase/migrations/* and frames promotion as a wrapper 'around existing component-library create behavior,' so the default execution path the plan describes IS two transactions, and the plan never states the orphan-on-partial-failure consequence of choosing the non-transaction branch. That is a real but HIGH (not CRITICAL) planning gap, not a false factual claim about the codebase.

### [CRITICAL] A7-F4 — CRITICAL: Idempotency guard (metadata.promotedLibraryItemId) is written in step 2, so a partial failure leaves an unguarded orphaned library item  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 02-...md 'Express To Library-Wide' + 04-...md U7 + AE6
- **Plan claim:** Promotion must be idempotent; use promotedLibraryItemId guard before create; retry after partial failure must not create duplicate workspace components (AE6).
- **Ground truth:** Guard is set only on relink; create RPC has no correlation_id/upsert, so create-then-crash leaves no guard and a retry double-creates.
- **Detail:** The plan's de-dup mechanism is 'a promotion correlation id or metadata.expressComponent.promotedLibraryItemId guard before create' (data-model doc) and 'rejects already-promoted rows unless retrying the same correlation id' (U7). But promotedLibraryItemId can only be written onto the express row during the relink (step 2 = update_estimate_row_item_with_history). If step 1 (create) succeeds and step 2 fails/crashes, the guard is NEVER set: the row still reads promotedLibraryItemId:null, so a retry's 'guard before create' check passes and create_cost_library_component runs AGAIN, producing a SECOND orphaned library component. create_cost_library_component has no correlation_id parameter and no ON CONFLICT idempotency on the item insert (it uses gen_random_uuid() for new_item_id when no id is supplied; the only ON CONFLICT in the RPC is the supplier upsert at migration line 198). So the naive guard does not actually prevent duplicates across the exact partial-failure window the plan claims to defend. AE6 ('repeat promotion does not duplicate the library component') is therefore not satisfiable by the described design without more machinery.
- **Evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260524141347_component_library_identity_variant_sku_brand_view.sql:142, supabase/migrations/20260524141347_component_library_identity_variant_sku_brand_view.sql:196-201, src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:448-452, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2571-2580
- **Fix:** Specify a concrete idempotency key that survives partial failure: derive a DETERMINISTIC library item id from the express row id + a stored promotion correlation id, persist that correlation id on the express row BEFORE calling create, and either (a) make create_cost_library_component accept the id and do INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING RETURNING (a migration), or (b) add an explicit SELECT-by-deterministic-id-before-create lookup. Note option (b) still has a TOCTOU race under concurrent promotion (see A7-F5). Update AE6 to assert behavior across the create-succeeds/relink-fails path, not just double-click.
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→high) — The mechanism is CONFIRMED accurate. create_cost_library_component (supabase/migrations/20260524141347:131-135) takes only (target_workspace_id, component, price_options) — NO correlation_id parameter (grep for correlation in that file returns nothing). new_item_id is gen_random_uuid() when no id supplied (line 142). The insert into cost_library_items (lines 203-254) has NO ON CONFLICT clause; the ONLY 'on conflict' in the entire RPC/file is the component_suppliers upsert at line 198 (on conflict (workspace_id, normalized_name)) — exactly as the finding states. The guard metadata.expressComponent.promotedLibraryItemId can only be written by the relink step (update_estimate_row_item_with_history), and that RPC replaces metadata wholesale at 20260524033151:479. So a create-success/relink-fail window leaves the row's promotedLibraryItemId unset and the create has no idempotency key, meaning a retry's guard-before-create check passes and create runs again -> second orphaned item. AE6 (04-implementation-plan.md:128) is therefore not satisfiable by the bare 'guard before create' design across the partial-failure window. I DOWNGRADE severity from critical to HIGH for two reasons: (1) the plan itself names the safer alternative — data-model line 63 and U7 line 110 say to PREFER a single RPC/transaction, which closes this exact window if chosen; and (2) the impl-plan and QA already flag the duplicate risk (RSK8 at 06-review:24, Q21 at 05-readiness:37, edge case at 05-readiness:84). So this is a design-incompleteness gap the plan partially anticipates, not an undefended unknown. It is real and must be resolved before shipping the non-transaction branch, but it is not an unacknowledged critical.

### [HIGH] A7-F5 — Concurrent promotion of the same express row can double-create (TOCTOU) with the lookup-before-create fallback  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 02-...md 'Express To Library-Wide' / U7
- **Plan claim:** Promotion must be idempotent ... a retry after partial failure must not create duplicate workspace components.
- **Ground truth:** No DB-level uniqueness or advisory lock prevents two concurrent creates; relink is serialized but library-create is not.
- **Detail:** The 'idempotent retry lookup before create' fallback is a check-then-act with no lock spanning the two RPCs. cost_library_items has no unique constraint that would reject a second component with the same name/provenance (the unique indexes are on (id, workspace_id) and supplier/folder identity — migration 20260523120957:87-92 / 20260524141347 indexes — not on a promotion key). Two concurrent promotion requests for the same express row (double-click, two tabs, retry overlapping the original) both pass the lookup, both call create_cost_library_component, and both insert distinct gen_random_uuid() items. The estimate-row relink half is serialized (update_estimate_row_item_with_history takes FOR UPDATE on the estimates row at migration 20260524033151:434 and on the row at :445, and enforces expected_row_revision at :451-459), so only one relink wins — but the library side is already double-created. The plan does not mention concurrency on the library-create side at all.
- **Evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:434-459, supabase/migrations/20260523120957_component_library_item_versioning.sql:87-99, src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:448-452
- **Fix:** Add a uniqueness/locking story: either a partial unique index on cost_library_items keyed by a promotion correlation column (migration), a pg_advisory_xact_lock keyed on the express row id inside a combined RPC, or a deterministic-id INSERT ... ON CONFLICT. Without one of these, concurrency safety is asserted but not achievable. List 'concurrent promotion' explicitly as a tested case in U7.
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→high) — CONFIRMED and severity is right. The unique indexes on cost_library_items are (id, workspace_id) (20260523120957:87-88), component_suppliers (id, workspace_id) (:89-90), library_folders (id, workspace_id) (:91-92), and the default-active price-option partial unique index (:99-101) — NONE is a promotion/provenance key that would reject a second component created from the same express row. So two concurrent create_cost_library_component calls each insert a distinct gen_random_uuid() item with no DB-level rejection (TOCTOU on the lookup-before-create fallback). The relink half IS serialized: update_estimate_row_item_with_history takes `for update` on the estimates row at 20260524033151:434 and on the row item at :445, and enforces expected_row_revision at :451-459 — so only one relink wins, but both library items already exist. The cited evidence lines are all accurate (note the finding labels the row lock :445 and revision check :451-459, which match). The plan's idempotency language (U7 line 110, data-model line 63) only addresses sequential 'retry after partial failure' and never mentions concurrency / two-tab / double-click on the library-create side, so this is a genuine uncovered gap. Keeping at HIGH (not critical) because it requires a concurrent race window and the single-RPC-with-lock option the plan prefers would also mitigate it; but it is a distinct vector from F4's sequential case and is correctly unaddressed by the plan.

### [MEDIUM] A7-F6 — Relink-via-update needs fields the action wrapper does not expose; RPC supports them but updateEstimateRowItem must be widened  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-...md U7 / KTD4
- **Plan claim:** relink the source row to the new library item with fresh snapshot, link_state='linked-clean', and row metadata provenance (using existing update RPC surface).
- **Ground truth:** RPC supports the relink patch; the existing action wrapper does not pass these fields and force-sets linked-overridden when a source id exists.
- **Detail:** U7 relinks the source row to set source_library_item_id, link_state='linked-clean', a fresh source_snapshot, and metadata provenance. The RPC update_estimate_row_item_with_history DOES accept all of these in row_patch (sourceLibraryItemId at migration 20260524033151:477, sourceSnapshot at :478, linkState at :474, metadata at :479). However the existing ACTION wrapper updateEstimateRowItem (src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2561) only forwards quantity/contingencyPercentage/miscCost and force-sets linkState to 'linked-overridden' when source_library_item_id is present (actions.ts:2568) — the opposite of what promotion needs. So promotion cannot reuse updateEstimateRowItem as-is; it needs a new/relink-specific action (or U3's widened patch) and must NOT inherit the linked-overridden override. The plan implies reuse of 'existing make-project-specific flow' and existing update surface but does not flag that the existing update action's link-state behavior actively conflicts with the relink goal.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2561-2580, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2568, supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:474-479
- **Fix:** In U7 (and cross-ref U3), specify a dedicated relink action that passes sourceLibraryItemId/sourceSnapshot/linkState/metadata and bypasses the 2568 linked-overridden auto-set. Add a test asserting the relinked row ends in linked-clean, not linked-overridden.

### [HIGH] A7-F7 — metadata is fully REPLACED by the update RPC — relink can clobber expressComponent provenance unless server-merged  _(type: risk)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-...md U7 / KTD7 / 02-...md Supabase Requirements bullet 4
- **Plan claim:** relink ... writes a fresh source_snapshot and row metadata provenance (KTD7 server-merge applies).
- **Ground truth:** RPC replaces metadata and source_snapshot wholesale on patch.
- **Detail:** update_estimate_row_item_with_history sets metadata = coalesce(row_patch->'metadata', '{}') when the patch carries metadata (migration 20260524033151:479) — a full replace, not a merge. The promotion relink must write metadata.expressComponent.promotedLibraryItemId, but if the client/action sends only the promotion delta, it WIPES the rest of metadata.expressComponent (kind, createdFrom, projectScoped) and any tags. This is the same hazard the plan flags in KTD7 / data-model 'Current row update RPC behavior replaces JSON values', but U7 does not restate it for the promotion path specifically, and it is the path most likely to lose the very provenance the feature depends on. Same applies to source_snapshot (line 478 is also a replace).
- **Evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:478-479, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2561-2580
- **Fix:** Explicitly require the promotion relink to read the current row server-side and write a COMPLETE merged metadata (preserving kind/createdFrom/projectScoped + tags + adding promotedLibraryItemId) and a complete source_snapshot. Add a test asserting unrelated metadata keys survive promotion, mirroring U3's test but for the promotion path.
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→high) — CONFIRMED with precise line evidence. update_estimate_row_item_with_history performs a FULL REPLACE of metadata: `metadata = case when row_patch ? 'metadata' then coalesce(row_patch->'metadata', '{}'::jsonb) else row_item.metadata end` at supabase/migrations/20260524033151:479 — no jsonb merge (`||`) and no jsonb_set. (The `||` merges I found at :924/:1408/:1646 are on the ESTIMATES table metadata, a different function/table, so the update RPC genuinely has no merge path for row metadata.) source_snapshot is likewise a full replace at :478. So if the relink patch sends only the promotion delta, it clobbers the rest of metadata.expressComponent (kind/createdFrom/projectScoped) and tags. The plan DOES flag this hazard generally — KTD7 at 04-implementation-plan.md:39, U3 at :81 ('the server action reads the current row and writes complete merged metadata and source_snapshot'), data-model at 02:116, and QA Q19 at 05-readiness:35 all require server-side merge. The finding's precise complaint is accurate: U7 (the promotion path, :110) does NOT restate the merge requirement, and it is the path most likely to lose the very provenance the feature depends on. So the underlying RPC behavior is exactly as described and the U7 omission is real. I keep HIGH: the mitigation pattern is already mandated elsewhere in the same plan, so it is a consistency/cross-reference gap in U7 rather than an undiscovered defect — the plan would not be correct to call it covered, but the merge discipline is established as a binding requirement (KTD7/U3/Q19) the implementer must apply to U7 too.

### [MEDIUM] A7-F8 — Relink revision conflict is a real, unhandled promotion failure mode  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-...md U7 / R11
- **Plan claim:** Promotion is idempotent and relinks the originating row (R11); retry after partial failure does not duplicate.
- **Ground truth:** Relink enforces row revision and throws P0001 on mismatch; a long create window makes conflict likely under active editing.
- **Detail:** update_estimate_row_item_with_history requires expected_row_revision and raises estimate_sheet_row_revision_conflict (errcode P0001) if the row changed since read (migration 20260524033151:451-459). Promotion's read-then-create-then-relink window is long (it spans a full library-create RPC). If another edit bumps the express row's revision between the read and the relink, the relink throws a revision conflict AFTER the library component already exists — landing in exactly the orphaned-component partial state of A7-F3/F4. The plan mentions idempotency but never names relink-revision-conflict as a failure mode or a retry trigger, even though the prompt calls it out.
- **Evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:451-459, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2575
- **Fix:** Define relink-conflict handling: on P0001, re-read the row, re-resolve the promotion correlation/guard (so the already-created library item is reused, not re-created), and retry the relink only. Add this to U7 tests. This is another argument for the single combined RPC (A7-F3) which would lock the row before creating the component.

### [LOW] A7-F9 — Promotion success but library-cache/revalidate path differs from estimate-cache invalidation — stale Components view risk  _(type: risk)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-...md U7 tests
- **Plan claim:** promotion ... updates Components workspace library cache (U7 test).
- **Ground truth:** Library create revalidates library/components routes; estimate row link-state is a separate cache with no shared invalidation on promotion.
- **Detail:** createCostLibraryComponent calls revalidateLibraryRoutes() (actions.ts:455 -> revalidatePath('/workshop/library'), '/workshop/components' at actions.ts:162-164) but the estimate-row relink uses the estimate domain's own invalidation. U7 says promotion 'updates Components workspace library cache' but the two halves invalidate different route/query caches and there is no single post-promotion invalidation that refreshes BOTH the Components library tree AND the estimate sheet's link-state badge. After a successful promotion the estimate row may still render as express/unlinked until its own query refetches. Minor but worth an explicit invalidation step in U7.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:162-164, src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:455
- **Fix:** Specify the full cache fan-out after promotion: invalidate the estimate-sheet row query (so the row flips to linked-clean immediately) AND the workspace component-library lite/rows query AND library routes. Add an assertion that the source estimate row shows linked state post-promotion without a manual refresh.

### [LOW] A7-F10 — Live Provider Evidence lists only a price-options READ policy; promotion writes rely on SECURITY DEFINER bypass (true but unstated)  _(type: missing-evidence)_
- **Plan loc:** 02-...md 'Live Provider Evidence'
- **Plan claim:** cost_library_item_price_options: RLS enabled, workspace member read policy; no new provider blast radius.
- **Ground truth:** Price-option writes during promotion work via SECURITY DEFINER on create_cost_library_component plus the app-layer assertCanManageLibrary editor gate (actions.ts:446), not via an INSERT RLS policy.
- **Detail:** The data-model 'Live Provider Evidence' table lists cost_library_item_price_options with only a 'workspace member read policy'. Promotion inserts price options. That insert succeeds only because create_cost_library_component is SECURITY DEFINER (migration 20260524141347:138) and runs as the function owner, bypassing the absence of an INSERT policy. The plan's conclusion ('avoids new provider blast radius') is correct for promotion, but the evidence table's read-only policy listing could read as if writes are blocked. The mechanism (definer bypass) is load-bearing and unstated.
- **Evidence:** 02-data-model-and-supabase-research.md:127, supabase/migrations/20260524141347_component_library_identity_variant_sku_brand_view.sql:138, src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:446
- **Fix:** Add one sentence to the evidence section noting price-option writes flow through the SECURITY DEFINER create RPC gated by assertCanManageLibrary (is_workspace_editor), so no new write policy is needed. This makes the no-new-migration claim defensible rather than implied.

### [LOW] A7-F11 — Promotion bypasses the workspace component quota check unless the wrapper re-invokes it  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-...md U7 / KTD4
- **Plan claim:** Promotion creates normal library components through existing create behavior.
- **Ground truth:** Existing create path enforces a 10k component quota in the action layer, not the RPC.
- **Detail:** createCostLibraryComponent enforces a per-workspace component cap via assertWithinLibraryQuota (actions.ts:447, LIBRARY_COMPONENT_CAP=10_000 at actions.ts:202). If U7 builds a thin promotion wrapper that calls createCostLibraryComponent directly, the quota is preserved automatically — good. But if the team instead writes a NEW combined promotion RPC (the recommended A7-F3 atomic path), that RPC must re-implement the quota head-count, or promotion becomes an unbounded create channel that bypasses the cap the rest of the library respects. The plan does not mention the quota at all.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:447, src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:202, src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:205-220
- **Fix:** Add a note: if promotion reuses createCostLibraryComponent the quota is inherited; if a combined RPC is introduced, port the quota check (or move it into the RPC) so promotion cannot bypass LIBRARY_COMPONENT_CAP.

**Missing documents proposed by this lane:**
- **U7 Promotion Atomicity & Idempotency Spec** — Resolve the critical gap: define exactly how express->library promotion stays atomic-or-recoverable and duplicate-free across the create + relink boundary, since no existing transaction spans both domains.
    - Decision: single combined SECURITY DEFINER RPC (promote_express_row_to_library_component) vs two-call saga — and the explicit acknowledgement this needs a new migration (contradicting R9 'no migration by default').
    - Deterministic library item id / promotion correlation id design: where it is persisted on the express row, written BEFORE create, and how it survives a create-succeeds/relink-fails crash.
    - Create-side de-dup: INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING RETURNING, or advisory lock on express row id, to defeat concurrent promotion (A7-F5).
    - Relink revision-conflict handling: on P0001 re-read row, reuse already-created component via guard, retry relink only (A7-F8).
    - Server-merged metadata + source_snapshot rules for the relink so expressComponent provenance/tags survive (A7-F7); link_state must land on linked-clean, not the auto linked-overridden (A7-F6).
    - Quota handling if a combined RPC is used (A7-F11).
    - Cache fan-out after success: estimate-sheet row query + workspace library lite/rows query + library routes (A7-F9).
    - Test matrix: happy path (1 item+1 option, row relinked), double-click, create-succeeds/relink-fails retry, concurrent promotion, relink revision conflict, metadata-preservation.

---

## Lane B1-strategy-product — readiness 72/100
**Summary:** The Express Components plan pack is unusually well-grounded against the real codebase: the overwhelming majority of cited line numbers land within 0-2 lines of the claimed symbol, and the behaviors described are accurate. The primary decision — author express components as estimate_row_items with no new table — is sound and defensible: I verified the row shape already carries source/snapshot/link_state/revision/history/RLS, the update RPC already accepts material/description/supplier/unit/unitCost patches (migration 20260524033151...:465-479), and the promotion RPC create_cost_library_component already exists and is atomic (component + price options). The plan is also honest about its hardest constraints: the JSON-replace-not-merge hazard (KTD7) is real and correctly grounded at SQL line 478-479. The two strategic weaknesses are (1) Unit U5 "fix project-specific icon bug" is bolted onto a bug whose root cause the plan never actually pins down — and my read of the code suggests the optimistic clone already preserves the link fields, making the plan's stated cause list speculative; and (2) scope is broad for a "day-0 mission-critical" slice (8 units spanning inline editor, contract widening, projection, promotion, and an undiagnosed bug fix). The express-vs-blank distinction is real (it is keyed on material label + tags in isBlankEstimateRowItem) but has a subtle classification edge the plan does not fully close. Intent fidelity is high; the plan serves the user's "inline-authored, project-specific, visible in Components, promotable" ask faithfully and resists over-building a new catalog table.

**Dimension scores:** strategy 80; intent-fidelity 82; scope-discipline 62; decision-quality 74

**Strengths:**
- Primary 'no new table' decision is verified against ground truth: estimate_row_items already owns material/description/supplier/unit/unit_cost/misc_cost/quantity/source_snapshot/link_state/metadata/row_revision (live evidence + migration), and the update RPC already accepts those patches at supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:465-479 — so the app-layer-only conclusion is correct.
- The JSON-merge hazard (KTD7/U3/RSK3) is real and precisely grounded: the update RPC REPLACES source_snapshot and metadata wholesale when present (migration lines 478-479), exactly as the plan warns; this is the kind of trap most plans miss.
- The promotion path is real, not invented: createCostLibraryComponent at src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:431 (exact line) calls a single create_cost_library_component RPC that writes component + price options atomically, validating the mermaid node and KTD4.
- Line-number fidelity is high overall — most citations are within 0-2 lines of the actual symbol, and the cited regression tests (estimate-sheet-library-insert.test.tsx:247, cost-inputs.test.tsx:218/230) assert exactly what the plan claims, giving implementers accurate regression targets.
- The render of the project-specific action is purely data-derived (cells.tsx:202 = canMakeProjectSpecific(item) per row, no index/hover logic), which correctly supports the plan's KTD5 framing that the bug fix must come from persisted link fields, not duplicate-count special-casing.
- DESIGN.md constraints cited by the UX doc (rounded-xs, hairline #F2F2F2, regular Phosphor icons) are faithful to the real design system (DESIGN.md:324/356/360), and the material grid column minmax(160px,1fr) claim is exact (row-table.tsx:88).

**Findings:**

### [HIGH] B1-F1 — U5 fixes an undiagnosed bug; the plan's stated root causes are unverified and partly contradicted by the code  _(type: risk)_
- **Plan loc:** 01-current-state-audit.md (Project-Specific Icon Bug Surface, lines 56-73); 04-implementation-plan.md U5 (92-97) and KTD5 (37)
- **Plan claim:** Doc 01:66-73 lists four candidate causes for the 'project-specific icon only appears on the second instance' bug; U5 (doc 04:92-97) and KTD5 (doc 04:37) propose to fix it by ensuring each inserted library row has sourceLibraryItemId and linked linkState.
- **Ground truth:** cloneLibraryItemForEstimate (EstimateSheetContent.tsx:352-368) spreads ...libraryItem, so it PRESERVES sourceLibraryItemId and linkState; only id and numeric fields are overridden. The insert RPC always sets linkState = sourceLibraryItemId ? 'linked-clean' : 'unlinked' (actions.ts:2552). The action visibility at cells.tsx:202 is purely canMakeProjectSpecific(item) per row with no index/hover/count logic (cells.tsx:79-82). So the two listed causes about the clone dropping fields are not borne out; a real cause would have to be a field-less incoming libraryItem payload from a specific insertion path, or the insert/cache split (see B1-F2), neither of which the plan confirms.
- **Detail:** For a day-0 mission-critical pack, bundling a fix for a bug that has not actually been root-caused is a schedule and correctness risk: the 'fix' (normalize source linkage on insert) may not touch the real defect. The plan even hedges ('Add an explicit normalization step IF library rows arrive without link state'), which is a tell that the cause is unknown.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:352, src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:2552, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/cells.tsx:79, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/cells.tsx:202, /Users/philiplewis/Documents/archestimator/src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2552
- **Fix:** Before treating U5 as implementation-ready, require a reproduction unit test that captures the exact libraryItem payload from BOTH insertion entry points (library-panel click vs drag-drop) and asserts which one yields a row that fails canMakeProjectSpecific. Make U5's first deliverable a failing test that pins the cause; only then write the normalization. Consider splitting U5 out of the express-components feature entirely — it is a pre-existing bug, not part of inline authoring.
- **Adversarial verify:** `corrected` (sev→high) — Independently confirmed all of the finding's code-level claims, with one minor citation error and two substantive corrections that on balance STRENGTHEN the skepticism but warrant a 'corrected' verdict rather than a rubber-stamp 'confirmed'.

VERIFIED AS STATED:
(1) cloneLibraryItemForEstimate is at EstimateSheetContent.tsx:352 and spreads ...libraryItem at line 360, overriding only id and numeric fields (lines 361-367) -> it PRESERVES sourceLibraryItemId and linkState. Confirmed.
(2) The insert RPC sets linkState via `sourceLibraryItemId ? "linked-clean" : "unlinked"` -> this is at actions.ts:2552 (verified verbatim), NOT EstimateSheetContent.tsx:2552. The finding's PROSE attributes it correctly to actions.ts, but its evidence[] array lists 'EstimateSheetContent.tsx:2552', which does not exist (that file is only 2524 lines and contains no 'linked-clean'/'unlinked' literal). Minor evidence mislabel; substance correct.
(3) canMakeProjectSpecific (cells.tsx:79-81) and its sole gate at cells.tsx:202 have zero index/hover/count logic; it is purely Boolean(sourceLibraryItemId || linkState startsWith 'linked' || linkState==='source-changed'). Confirmed.
(4) Doc 01:66-73 does list four candidate causes (bullets at 68-71); KTD5 (doc 04:37) and U5 (doc 04:92-97) propose normalizing inserted-row source linkage; U5 line 96 contains the hedge 'Add an explicit normalization step if library rows arrive without link state but have a source id'. Confirmed.

CORRECTION 1 (finding slightly OVERSTATES that the plan ignores the real vector): The finding says a real cause 'would have to be a field-less incoming libraryItem payload from a specific insertion path, or the insert/cache split ... neither of which the plan confirms.' But the plan DID name this exact vector at doc 01:69 ('the optimistic insert uses a cloned row locally but passes the un-cloned libraryItem to the action'), and that IS a real code fact: EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1727 passes the original un-cloned `libraryItem` (not the cloned `newItem` created at line 1706) into insertRow. So the plan is not blindly guessing; it identified the most plausible mechanism. 'Undiagnosed' (no confirmed repro) is fair, but 'partly contradicted by the code' is too strong about the clone -- the clone is irrelevant to the server payload precisely because the un-cloned item is what is sent.

CORRECTION 2 (a stronger point the finding MISSED, which understates the case AGAINST U5's fix): On refetch, the reconcile mapping at workspace-repository.ts:1675 sets `linkState: row.link_state` with NO fallback, unlike the insert path's `?? 'linked-clean'`. So a persisted row with null link_state reconciles to linkState:null. Yet canMakeProjectSpecific STILL returns true via the sourceLibraryItemId OR-branch (cells.tsx:81). Therefore normalizing linkState alone (U5's proposed fix) would NOT change icon visibility unless sourceLibraryItemId is ALSO null. This further undermines U5's stated remedy and narrows the true defect, reinforcing the finding's thesis. Additionally, getSourceLibraryItemId (actions.ts:782-785) has a fallback that derives the id from libraryItem.id when source==='library', making the server insert MORE robust than the plan implies -- so a row would only end up 'unlinked' if the incoming libraryItem has source!=='library' AND no sourceLibraryItemId, a narrow path the plan never pins down.

CONTEXT the finding under-credits: the plan's own risk register (doc 06, RSK4:20) rates this Medium, frames it explicitly as a hypothesis ('insert payload lacks link fields'), and its mitigation includes 'add duplicate-instance regression' -- i.e., U5 tests (doc 04:97) intend to build a reproducing test, the responsible way to confirm the cause.

NET: severity stays HIGH for a day-0 mission-critical pack -- bundling a fix for an unreproduced bug whose proposed remedy plausibly misses the real defect (per Correction 2) is a genuine correctness/schedule risk. Verdict 'corrected' because the finding's framing overstates the clone contradiction while missing the stronger reconcile-path argument.

### [MEDIUM] B1-F2 — Insert path mutates local React state while detach mutates the TanStack cache — a real reconciliation split the plan only hints at  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 01-current-state-audit.md:71; U5 04-implementation-plan.md:92-97
- **Plan claim:** Doc 01:71 lists 'The query key updated by the detach mutation differs from the visible tree query key for tabbed estimates' as one possible bug cause, but the plan does not analyze the deeper insert-vs-cache state split.
- **Ground truth:** insertLibraryItem optimistically updates local component state via setEstimateTree (EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1708), and the insert mutation's onSuccess (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:142-146) only markSaved + invalidateQueries with refetchType:'none' — it never reconciles the void-returning server insert (actions.ts:2558 returns nothing) back into any tree. By contrast detachMutation writes directly to queryClient.setQueryData(queryKey,...) (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:188-195, 201-203). So inserted rows live in local state until a real refetch, while detach edits the cache.
- **Detail:** This dual source of truth (local estimateTree vs TanStack cache) is the most plausible real mechanism behind any 'state only updates on some instances' symptom, and it directly affects U2's 'insert must return persisted row readiness and row revision' requirement: today the insert action returns void, so there is no persisted revision to buffer against. U2/U10's pending-insert contract is therefore not just a UI buffer change — it requires changing insertEstimateRowItemFromLibrary to RETURN the persisted row, which the plan does not call out as a server-action signature change.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1708, src/hooks/use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:142, src/hooks/use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:188, /Users/philiplewis/Documents/archestimator/src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2558
- **Fix:** Add an explicit unit (or extend U2) to change insertEstimateRowItemFromLibrary to return the persisted row (id + row_revision + reconciled fields) and to reconcile it into BOTH the local estimateTree and the cache, eliminating the dual-source split. State plainly that R10/AE2a's 'wait for persisted row revision' is impossible until this signature changes, since the action currently returns void.

### [MEDIUM] B1-F3 — Express-vs-blank classification has an unclosed edge: an express row with an empty/default name can still match isBlankEstimateRowItem  _(type: risk)_
- **Plan loc:** 02-data-model-and-supabase-research.md:97-99 (inclusion/exclusion rules); 04-implementation-plan.md U1 (61-66); evidence/subagent-summaries.md:19
- **Plan claim:** The pack's central premise is that express is NOT 'just a prettier blank placeholder' (evidence/subagent-summaries.md:19) and that express rows are distinguishable from blanks via metadata.expressComponent (KTD2, doc 04:34). U1 (doc 04:65) keeps isBlankEstimateRowItem for legacy placeholders and adds express helpers.
- **Ground truth:** isBlankEstimateRowItem (activity-history.tsx:120-134) returns true when source==='project' AND material===BLANK_ESTIMATE_ROW_COMPONENT_LABEL AND (tagged placeholder OR (itemCost===0 && category==='Unassigned' && !imageUrl)). It is a heuristic on material label + tag, NOT a metadata-driven check. A freshly created express row that starts with the placeholder label or with an empty/default name, zero cost, and Unassigned category could still be classified as blank by EVERY existing caller (cells.tsx:179 blank slot render, :80 canMakeProjectSpecific gate, activity-history audit labels) until the user types a real name.
- **Detail:** The plan's exclusion rule 'Exclude rows where isBlankEstimateRowItem would still classify the row as the Add row placeholder' (doc 02:98) assumes express rows reliably escape blank classification, but the new express template's default material is unspecified (doc 04:75 only says 'non-placeholder material default'). If the express template seeds material='' or the placeholder label, the row is born mis-classified and will render as a blank dashed slot instead of the editor (cells.tsx:179), and will be excluded from the Project folder projection.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/activity-history.tsx:120, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/cells.tsx:80, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/cells.tsx:179
- **Fix:** Make U1 the authoritative gate: express identity MUST be the presence of metadata.expressComponent (or a dedicated tag), and isBlankEstimateRowItem MUST be narrowed to exclude any row carrying express metadata REGARDLESS of material/cost/category. Specify the express template's default material explicitly and require it to fail isBlankEstimateRowItem on creation. Add a unit test for 'express row with empty name is NOT classified blank'.

### [LOW] B1-F4 — The audit understates the existing update RPC capability, mildly skewing the build estimate  _(type: inaccuracy)_
- **Plan loc:** 01-current-state-audit.md:44-54; 04-implementation-plan.md U3 (78-82)
- **Plan claim:** Doc 01:44-54 frames the update contract as 'too narrow for express component authoring' and says 'Express editing needs a typed patch'; doc 04:80 lists supabase/migrations/* changes 'only if RPC payload validation needs a compatibility change'. Doc 01:52 only credits the RPC with supporting sourceSnapshot and metadata.
- **Ground truth:** The update_estimate_row_item_with_history RPC already supports patches for material, description, supplier, unit, unitCost, miscCost, discipline, designation, linkState, sourceLibraryItemId, sourceSnapshot, and metadata (migration 20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:465-479). The narrowing is entirely in the APP layer: UpdateEstimateRowItemInput (actions.ts:80-89) and toOptimisticRowPatch (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:70-76) only expose numeric fields. No migration is needed for the material-field widening.
- **Detail:** This is the plan's conclusion direction (app-layer only), so it is not a wrong decision — but the audit doc credits the RPC with less than it actually does, and U3's mention of 'supabase/migrations/* only if needed' leaves a false impression that DB work might be on the path. Being precise here de-risks the migration gate and removes a phantom scope item.
- **Evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:465, supabase/migrations/20260524033151_estimate_sheet_version_control.sql:468, src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:80, src/hooks/use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:70
- **Fix:** Update doc 01 to state explicitly that the RPC already accepts material/description/supplier/unit/unitCost/miscCost patches (cite lines 465-479), and change U3 to assert 'NO migration needed; the widening is app-layer typing + tree-patch + optimistic-patch only,' reserving the migration caveat solely for the JSON-merge semantics (which is a genuine open question).

### [MEDIUM] B1-F5 — Editing any express field on a row that still carries a source_library_item_id silently flips link_state to linked-overridden — unanalyzed for the promote-then-edit flow  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 04-implementation-plan.md U3 (78-82), U7 (106-111), KTD4 (36)
- **Plan claim:** U3 (doc 04:81) says 'Preserve expected revision checks and linked row override behavior.' KTD4/U7 relink the promoted row to link_state='linked-clean' and then expect the user to keep editing the express component.
- **Ground truth:** updateEstimateRowItem (actions.ts:2561-2569) unconditionally adds linkState:'linked-overridden' to the patch whenever row.source_library_item_id is present (line 2568). After U7 relinks a promoted express row to linked-clean with a source_library_item_id, the very next inline field edit through the same update path will force the row to linked-overridden — changing its render (it would now show the make-project-specific Cube action again per canMakeProjectSpecific, cells.tsx:81) and its meaning.
- **Detail:** The plan's promotion design assumes a relinked row behaves like a clean library instance, but the existing update action will immediately override it on edit, producing a confusing state where a just-promoted express component is treated as a customized library instance. The plan never reconciles 'relink to linked-clean' (U7) with 'force linked-overridden on edit' (existing action) — they conflict.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/estimates/actions.ts:2568, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/cells.tsx:81
- **Fix:** In U3, make the linked-overridden override conditional and express-aware (e.g., do not flip to linked-overridden for rows carrying metadata.expressComponent, or decide explicitly what post-promotion edits should do). In U7, state the intended post-promotion edit semantics. Add a test: promote express row, then edit a field, and assert the resulting link_state matches the documented intent.

### [LOW] B1-F6 — Alternatives A/B/C/D table is honest and non-strawman, but 'no new table' defers a fork with a higher near-term probability than the plan implies  _(type: inconsistency)_
- **Plan loc:** 02-data-model-and-supabase-research.md Alternatives (20-26), Open Product Fork (132-138); 06-review-and-risk-register.md RSK1 (17)
- **Plan claim:** The Alternatives table (doc 02:21-26) marks C (new project_components table) 'Defer' and the Open Product Fork (doc 02:132-138, RSK1) treats project-local reusable components as a future, low-probability need to gate later.
- **Ground truth:** The codebase already has first-class machinery for reusing one project's rows across estimates: TemplatesTree builds a project-source subtree (buildProjectSourceTreeNodes, TemplatesTree.tsx:235) and returns checked project-source row items (getCheckedProjectSourceRowItems region ~:524), and there is an insert_estimate_subtree_into_estimate RPC pair (per decisions.md 2026-06-13). The product ALREADY lets users pull rows from one project into another — i.e., cross-estimate reuse of project-scoped rows is a shipped concept, not hypothetical.
- **Detail:** Because cross-project row reuse already exists as a UX pattern, the user's likely next ask ('let me reuse this express component in another estimate without promoting it to the workspace library') is closer than 'a future product requirement.' The plan's projection-only approach (estimate_row_items + virtual folder) means each reuse is a fresh row id with a copied snapshot, not a canonical shared component — exactly the projection-not-catalog limitation the plan names at doc 02:136. The reasoning is honest, but the deferral's risk weighting (RSK1 'High' severity but treated as a later gate) under-weights how naturally the existing project-source feature invites the fork.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/component-library/tree/TemplatesTree.tsx:235, src/components/workshop/component-library/tree/TemplatesTree.tsx:524, /Users/philiplewis/Documents/archestimator/.ai/10_state/decisions.md:194
- **Fix:** Strengthen RSK1: note that project-source reuse already ships, so 'reuse an express component across estimates' is a probable near-term ask. Add an explicit, cheap forward-compatibility hedge now — e.g., a stable metadata.expressComponent.id (a project-local component identity) written at creation — so a future project_components catalog can adopt existing rows without a data backfill. This costs almost nothing today and avoids a painful migration if the fork lands.

### [MEDIUM] B1-F8 — Scope is broad for a 'day-0 mission-critical' slice; the inline-author core and the projection/promotion/bug-fix should be separable  _(type: risk)_
- **Plan loc:** INDEX.md (whole); 04-implementation-plan.md Implementation Units (60-118); 05-readiness-qa-matrix.md (13-37)
- **Plan claim:** INDEX.md frames this as a single mission-critical day-0 pack delivering all of: inline express editing (U2-U4), contract widening (U3), Components projection (U6), promotion (U7), bug fix (U5), and downstream verification (U8). The readiness matrix (doc 05) treats all 21 scenarios as one gate.
- **Ground truth:** The user's stated intent is an inline-authored project-specific component created in a row, visible in Components, promotable to library. The minimum to satisfy 'author inline + persist' is U1+U2+U3+U4. U6 (virtual Project folder projection with project-context plumbing), U7 (idempotent promotion with a new transaction boundary), and U5 (pre-existing bug) are each substantial and independently riskier — U7 in particular wants a NEW Postgres RPC/transaction for create-plus-relink that does not exist today (createCostLibraryComponent only creates the component, actions.ts:431-454).
- **Detail:** Bundling an undiagnosed bug (U5), a new projection surface (U6) with auth-boundary plumbing, and a new idempotent promotion RPC (U7) into one day-0 readiness gate raises the probability that the whole feature slips or ships with the weakest unit under-tested. The plan sequences well internally but presents one monolithic readiness bar.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:431, src/lib/component-library/actions.ts:448
- **Fix:** Re-tier the pack: Tier 1 (day-0 mission-critical) = U1-U4 (author + persist + edit inline) with its own ship gate; Tier 2 (fast-follow) = U6 projection and U7 promotion, each behind its own gate; treat U5 as a separate pre-existing-bug ticket. Make INDEX.md's Stop Condition tier-aware so 'ready to ship the core' is decoupled from 'ready to ship promotion'.

## Lane B2-ux-ui-accessibility — readiness 52/100
**Summary:** The Express Components pack is unusually citation-accurate (blank slot cells.tsx:179, project-specific gate cells.tsx:79, material column row-table.tsx:88, tree aria roles tree.tsx:1124+ all verified), and it correctly names the right accessibility mechanisms (labels, focus rings, aria-live, aria-invalid/aria-describedby, tree roles) which the existing primitives (ControlInput.invalid, ControlField.errorText) already support. However the design lens surfaces several real gaps. The single biggest implementability hole is the keyboard model: the plan states the arrow-nav-vs-caret conflict as a goal (03-ux:35) but gives zero concrete resolution, while the live handleEstimateCellKeyDown unconditionally preventDefaults ArrowLeft/Right/Enter and is wired to grid cells — express text fields must not use it, and nothing says how. Focus-on-insert collides with an existing pendingFocusRowId effect that hard-targets the quantity cell, not the name field the plan wants focused. Accessibility omits the WCAG AA contrast floor entirely while reusing #7F7F7F tertiary and secondary/60 for load-bearing meta (both fail AA), and the interaction-state set covers only ~half the required states while inventing a Creating indicator that does not exist in the 5-value save machine. No state or copy spec exists, so empty/error/announcement microcopy is undefined, and the expanding-editor-vs-virtualization rule is directional rather than concrete against the fixed 96px row.

**Dimension scores:** ux-coverage 58; accessibility 55; design-system-compliance 60; interaction-state-completeness 48

**Strengths:**
- Source-citation accuracy is high: spot-checked blank slot (cells.tsx:179), project-specific gate (cells.tsx:79-82), material column minmax(160px,1fr) (row-table.tsx:88), badge/action split (cells.tsx:201,276), and tree aria contract (tree.tsx:1124-1134,1260) all match the code at or near the cited lines.
- Accessibility section names the correct mechanisms and they are already supported by primitives: ControlInput/ControlTextarea accept invalid->aria-invalid (control-input.tsx:18,42) and ControlField has errorText (control-input.tsx:82), so aria-invalid/aria-describedby and inline error are buildable without new infra.
- Tree-role reuse claim is grounded: tree.tsx already wires role=treeitem/tree, aria-expanded/selected/current/grabbed and dropEffect, so the Components Project folder can inherit correct semantics.
- Existing sr-only aria-live status regions already exist in EstimateSheetContent (lines 600,932), giving a real pattern to extend for save/fail/promote announcements.
- Commit model (blur/Enter commit, Escape revert, buffered pre-insert commits with draft recovery) is coherent and matches the existing optimistic-update + rollback architecture.

**Findings:**

### [CRITICAL] B2-F1 — Arrow-nav vs text-caret conflict is named as a goal but has no concrete resolution; the live handler will hijack caret movement in express text fields  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 03-ux-interaction-design.md Commit model (35)
- **Plan claim:** Arrow navigation should continue to work for grid numeric cells and should not hijack text editing while caret movement is expected.
- **Ground truth:** handleEstimateCellKeyDown preventDefaults ArrowLeft/Right/Enter and moves cells; it is attached to numeric cells at row-table.tsx:254/310/345. No express-field carve-out exists.
- **Detail:** 03-ux:35 says arrow nav must keep working for numeric cells and must not hijack text editing, but no resolution is given. Ground truth: handleEstimateCellKeyDown (cells.tsx:45-73) unconditionally event.preventDefault()s ArrowLeft/Right (lines 59-60,66) to move cells and ArrowLeft would never reach the caret; Enter (52-55) blurs/commits; it is wired to all three numeric cells (row-table.tsx:254,310,345). If express name/description inputs reuse this handler, Left/Right caret movement, Home/End text selection, and Enter-for-newline (in the description textarea) all break. The conflict is real and the plan offers no selectionStart/selectionEnd boundary check, no separate keydown handler, and no statement that express fields opt out of the grid handler. The risk register (RSK1-9) also omits it.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/cells.tsx:45, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/cells.tsx:59, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:254, /Users/philiplewis/Documents/archestimator/.ai/03_plans/2026-06-15-Express-Components/03-ux-interaction-design.md:35
- **Fix:** Specify that express text fields do NOT use handleEstimateCellKeyDown. Define a dedicated keydown policy: Left/Right/Home/End pass through to the caret; ArrowUp/Down only move rows when the caret is at the field boundary (or never, for text fields); Enter commits for single-line inputs but inserts a newline in the description textarea (use Cmd/Ctrl+Enter or blur to commit there); Escape reverts. Capture this in a keyboard/focus spec and add per-key tests.
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→critical) — Ground truth holds exactly. handleEstimateCellKeyDown (src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/cells.tsx:45-73) unconditionally event.preventDefault()s Enter (line 53, then blurs/commits at 54) and ArrowRight/ArrowLeft/ArrowUp/ArrowDown (line 66, via keyMap 58-63) to move grid cells — there is NO selectionStart/selectionEnd or caret-boundary check anywhere in the function. It is wired to all three numeric cells: quantity onKeyDown (row-table.tsx:254), contingency-percentage (row-table.tsx:310), misc-cost MoneyInput (row-table.tsx:345). The original finding's cited line cells.tsx:45 (handler start) and cells.tsx:59 (ArrowRight/keyMap) are correct; line 59-60/66 preventDefault references map correctly (ArrowLeft is keyMap entry at 60, the single preventDefault is at 66 covering all arrows). I confirmed the plan offers no resolution: a full-pack rg for 'preventDefault|selectionStart|caret|hijack|separate keydown' returns nothing, and the only arrow-nav text is the bare goal statement at 03-ux-interaction-design.md:35. The risk register RSK1-RSK9 (06-review-and-risk-register.md:17-25) contains no arrow-nav/caret entry, and U4's keyboard contract (04-implementation-plan.md:88, 'Commit on blur/Enter') silently inherits the grid Enter-to-blur behavior, which would break Enter-for-newline in the proposed multi-line description textarea. The conflict is real, unresolved, and unregistered. Critical severity is appropriate for a day-0 surface users type into. Minor correction: if express fields are built as brand-new inputs that simply do NOT attach handleEstimateCellKeyDown, the conflict is avoidable — but the plan never states that opt-out, so the gap stands.

### [HIGH] B2-F2 — Load-bearing material meta reuses #7F7F7F tertiary which fails WCAG AA; contrast floor never mentioned  _(type: risk)_
- **Plan loc:** 03-ux-interaction-design.md Accessibility (78-86) and Layout Constraints (98)
- **Plan claim:** Accessibility requires labels, focus rings, aria-live, aria-invalid/describedby, tree roles; Layout requires neutral surfaces per DESIGN.md - no contrast floor stated.
- **Ground truth:** Existing material meta renders at #7F7F7F (~3.5:1) and #5D5D5D@60% (~2.6:1) on white at 10-11px; both fail WCAG AA 4.5:1.
- **Detail:** The express editor edits fields the current card renders in failing contrast: supplier/unit use text-tertiary=#7F7F7F (cells.tsx:96,107) and description uses text-secondary/60 (#5D5D5D@60% over white ~ #9E9E9E, cells.tsx:104). #7F7F7F on white is ~3.5:1 and #9E9E9E ~2.6:1 - both below 4.5:1 AA for 10-11px text. DESIGN.md pins tertiary:#7F7F7F (line 9; globals.css:12) and sanctions it for metadata/descriptions (line 341), but per the project's own standard it fails AA for load-bearing meta and #5D5D5D should be used. The plan's Accessibility section (03-ux:78-86) and 'neutral surfaces per DESIGN.md' layout note (03-ux:98) never mention a contrast floor, so the editor inherits and amplifies the defect on a surface users type into daily.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/cells.tsx:96, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/cells.tsx:104, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/cells.tsx:107, src/app/globals.css:12, /Users/philiplewis/Documents/archestimator/.ai/03_plans/2026-06-15-Express-Components/03-ux-interaction-design.md:78
- **Fix:** Add a requirement: editable/load-bearing express text (name, description, unit, supplier, unit cost) renders at #5D5D5D secondary or stronger (#3D3D3D ink for the active value), never tertiary #7F7F7F and never secondary/60. Reserve #7F7F7F for non-load-bearing decoration only. Note this corrects an existing cells.tsx defect, and add a contrast check to QA browser smoke.
- **Adversarial verify:** `corrected` (sev→high) — Core claim confirmed but one number is wrong and the finding is actually UNDERSTATED on the project's own self-awareness. Verified colors: --tertiary:#7f7f7f (globals.css:12; DESIGN.md:9) is used for supplier (cells.tsx:96) and unit (cells.tsx:107) at text-[10px]; description uses text-secondary/60 (cells.tsx:104) which composites #5d5d5d at 60% over white = exactly #9E9E9E. Recomputed WCAG ratios (sRGB): #7F7F7F on white = 4.0:1 (NOT the ~3.5:1 the finding states — correction; still below the 4.5:1 AA floor for sub-18px text, so the conclusion stands), and #9E9E9E on white = 2.68:1 (finding's ~2.6:1 is accurate). #5D5D5D = 6.58:1, which passes. DESIGN.md:341 does sanction #7F7F7F for metadata/descriptions, confirming the finding's framing. CRITICAL ADDITION the finding missed: the codebase has ALREADY litigated this exact defect — globals.css:22-26 defines --muted-foreground:#6b6b6b with an inline comment stating '#6b6b6b ≈ 5.3:1 on white — clears WCAG 1.4.3 (4.5:1) for body text, while the design-system-pinned --tertiary (#7f7f7f) stays untouched' and references prior finding '06 A11Y-2/16'. So the project explicitly acknowledges #7F7F7F fails AA and keeps it only by pinning. This strengthens the finding: the express plan edits these very fields and its Accessibility section (03-ux:78-86) and layout note (03-ux:98, 'neutral surfaces per DESIGN.md') name no contrast floor and ignore the existing #6b6b6b precedent. High severity is correct (not critical: it is an inherited-defect amplification on metadata text, WCAG AA fail, not a functional break).

### [HIGH] B2-F3 — Interaction-state set is incomplete and invents a Creating indicator absent from the 5-value save machine  _(type: inaccuracy)_
- **Plan loc:** 03-ux-interaction-design.md Commit model (36) and Visual states (40)
- **Plan claim:** Dirty/saving/saved/failed states reuse saveState; new row commit indicator shows Creating until persisted row readiness.
- **Ground truth:** EstimateSaveState has 5 values and no Creating/insert state; it is hook-global, not per-field (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:11,103-122,304).
- **Detail:** 03-ux:36 says dirty/saving/saved/failed reuse saveState from use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:11, and 03-ux:40 says the new row shows a Creating indicator. Ground truth: the export is the TYPE EstimateSaveState='idle'|'dirty'|'saving'|'saved'|'failed' (line 11) - 5 states, no Creating/insert state; saveState is a single hook-global value (useState at 104, returned 304, consumed once at EstimateSheetContent.tsx:996,1642), not per-field/per-row. The Visual States list (03-ux:38-46) covers ~6 states (new/clean/dirty/failed/detached/linked) and omits loading, empty pre-keystroke, saved-confirm-and-timeout, in-flight-vs-queued saving, detached-but-editing, linked-overridden editing, source-changed editor behavior, promoting, and draft-insert-failed-while-editing. The required ~14-state set is roughly half covered and the one new state name does not map to code.
- **Evidence:** /Users/philiplewis/Documents/archestimator/.ai/03_plans/2026-06-15-Express-Components/03-ux-interaction-design.md:36, /Users/philiplewis/Documents/archestimator/.ai/03_plans/2026-06-15-Express-Components/03-ux-interaction-design.md:40, src/hooks/use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:11, src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:996
- **Fix:** Either extend the state machine with an explicit insert/creating state plus per-row save tracking (and say so in U2/U3), or drop Creating and derive the insert-pending indicator from the buffered-insert flag. Add a State Spec enumerating all states with visual + aria-live behavior.
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→high) — Confirmed with one small citation cleanup. EstimateSaveState is a 5-value union type 'idle'|'dirty'|'saving'|'saved'|'failed' (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:11) — there is NO 'Creating'/'insert'/'inserting' state, so 03-ux:40's 'commit indicator shows Creating until persisted row readiness' names a state absent from the save machine and unmapped to code. Confirmed it is hook-global, not per-field/per-row: useSaveState (use-estimate-row-mutations.ts:103-122) holds a single useState (104) with markRowDirty/markSaving/markSaved/markFailed mutating one value; consumers verified — destructured once in EstimateSheetContent at line 996 and read only inside the editable-rows-sync effect (1642, 1650). Citation correction: the finding lists '996,1642' as two consumption sites, but 1642 and 1650 are the same single useEffect, and 996 is the destructure; there is exactly one stateful consumer, which reinforces (not weakens) the 'one hook-global value' point. 03-ux:36's claim that dirty/saving/saved/failed 'reuse saveState' is technically true the value exists, but the plan never reconciles a single hook-global flag with the per-field/per-row dirty/saved/failed indicators the Visual States list (03-ux:38-46) implies — a real design gap. The finding's enumeration of ~14 missing states (loading, empty pre-keystroke, saved-confirm-timeout, queued-vs-in-flight saving, detached-but-editing, linked-overridden editing, source-changed editor, promoting, draft-insert-failed-while-editing) is a reasonable spec critique; I confirmed the doc's list at 38-46 covers only ~6 states. High severity appropriate (state-model incompleteness plus a phantom state name; not critical since it is a planning-completeness defect, not a code break).

### [HIGH] B2-F4 — Focus-on-insert collides with existing pendingFocusRowId effect that hard-targets the quantity cell  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 03-ux-interaction-design.md Visual states (40); 04 U2 pending-insert contract (73)
- **Plan claim:** New row: active express editor with name focused; the UI may focus the new name field immediately.
- **Ground truth:** pendingFocusRowId effect targets cell-index 0 = quantity input and selects it (EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1652-1664); the name field has no cell-index, so the effect cannot focus it and would override name focus.
- **Detail:** 03-ux:40 and 04 U2 say the express row appears with the name focused, but the existing insert focus mechanism pendingFocusRowId (EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1021,1707) focuses [data-estimate-row-id][data-estimate-cell-index=0] - the QUANTITY input (row-table.tsx:257) - and calls .select()+smooth scrollIntoView (1658-1662). The express name field will not carry a numeric data-estimate-cell-index (that scheme is for grid arrow-nav cells), so the current effect cannot target it and would steal focus to quantity right after the user starts typing the name. Neither the UX doc nor U2/U4 mention redirecting this effect or how the name field participates in the cell-index focus model. This is the most likely day-0 focus regression.
- **Evidence:** src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1652, src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1658, src/components/workshop/EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1707, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:257, /Users/philiplewis/Documents/archestimator/.ai/03_plans/2026-06-15-Express-Components/03-ux-interaction-design.md:40
- **Fix:** Specify in U2/U4 that the pending-focus effect branches on express rows to target the express name input (e.g., data-express-field=name), suppresses quantity-cell focus for express inserts, and defines post-commit/post-Escape focus. Add focus-on-insert and focus-after-commit assertions to QA Q1.
- **Adversarial verify:** `confirmed` (sev→high) — Confirmed precisely. The pendingFocusRowId effect (EstimateSheetContent.tsx:1652-1674) hard-targets [data-estimate-row-id][data-estimate-cell-index='0'] (line 1658) and calls scrollIntoView({behavior:'smooth'}) + focus() + select() (1660-1662). Cell-index 0 is the quantity ControlInput (row-table.tsx:255-257). The effect is triggered on optimistic insert: insertLibraryItem calls setPendingFocusRowId(newItem.id) at line 1707 (state declared at 1021), retrying across up to 5 animation frames (1668). The express name field will not carry a numeric data-estimate-cell-index (that attribute scheme exists only for grid arrow-nav numeric cells per cells.tsx:25-43 and row-table.tsx:255/311/346), so this effect cannot target the name field and WILL steal focus to the quantity input after insert resolves — a concrete day-0 focus regression. The plan promises name-focus at 03-ux:40, R10 (04:28), U2 pending-insert contract (04:73 'UI may focus the new name field immediately'), and acceptance AE1 (04:122 'new row appears with focused component name') — but nowhere does it mention redirecting/disabling the pendingFocusRowId effect or how the name field joins the cell-index focus model. Verified the full pack: no rg hit for 'pendingFocus' or 'cell-index'/'cellIndex' anywhere. High is correct; I would not raise to critical only because the express insert may use a different insert path than insertLibraryItem (the Add-row express template is U2, distinct from library insert), so whether setPendingFocusRowId fires for express rows depends on implementation — but the existing pattern at 1707 is the obvious one to copy, and the collision is unaddressed either way.

- **Fix:** State in U4 that the express editor's dirty/active/replace/focus affordances use accent-soft (#F5F5F5)+accent ring per DESIGN.md, and note that the surrounding blue replace/active styling is a pre-existing inconsistency to either align or knowingly leave; do not silently extend the blue into new code.

### [MEDIUM] B2-F8 — Expanding editor height vs virtualization: truncation/expansion rule is directional, not concrete  _(type: gap)_
- **Plan loc:** 03-ux-interaction-design.md Layout Constraints (94-96); RSK7 in 06
- **Plan claim:** Allow active edit state to expand only within defined row height rules, or disable virtualization assumptions for active rows.
- **Ground truth:** Rows are fixed min-h-24/96px with estimateSize 96 and contain-intrinsic-size:96px + content-visibility:auto (row-table.tsx:185,418-428); an expanding active row will mismeasure/clip and material col min is 160px.
- **Detail:** 03-ux:94-96 says keep height stable for virtualization, truncate in clean state, and allow active expansion within defined row-height rules or disable virtualization for active rows - but those rules are never defined. Ground truth: rows are fixed min-h-24 (96px) with virtualizer estimateSize hard-coded 96 (row-table.tsx:185,418-428,487), contain-intrinsic-size:96px and content-visibility:auto (line 185). A multi-line description textarea (03-ux:22) growing past 96px will be mismeasured (loosely RSK7) and content-visibility:auto can clip an expanded active row. No concrete max active height, no measureElement call, no opt-out of contain-intrinsic-size is given. At narrow widths (material col min 160px, row-table.tsx:88) name+supplier+unit-cost+rail (rail reserves pr-[92px], cells.tsx:94) is tight; 'avoid overlap' (line 96) lacks a layout strategy.
- **Evidence:** /Users/philiplewis/Documents/archestimator/.ai/03_plans/2026-06-15-Express-Components/03-ux-interaction-design.md:94, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:185, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:418, src/components/workshop/estimate-sheet/rows/row-table.tsx:88
- **Fix:** Define a concrete active-row max height (or exclude active rows from virtualization and use measureElement), specify that the active row drops contain-intrinsic-size/content-visibility, give the clean-state truncation rule (single-line name + clamped description), and provide a narrow-width layout strategy so 'avoid overlap' is testable.
